At run time a thermostat that is off gets its last mode and nothing else, so its setpoints and fan are the ones it kept; one that is on is left as it is (nothing sent, counted as ran); one whose last mode is not known yet is skipped with `NO_LAST_MODE` ("No last mode known yet; set it once (turn it on in a mode) and DirectorLink remembers it"); one whose last mode DirectorLink cannot set (Dry from the AC's own remote) with `MODE_NOT_SUPPORTED`. It never guesses heat or cool. Thermostats with heat and cool setpoints and floor heating that only heats work the same. The name is the owner's word, reads as what it does (`"mode": "on"`), and a 1.9.0 app shows it as "On" (it capitalizes a mode it has no word for); `fan_speed` `on` is another field. `features.climate_last_mode` in `GET /v1/system` says the driver has both.
- **Keep.** A climate step's fields left out stay as they are; the scene editor now says so: **Mode** Off, **On, as it was** (with a 1.10.0 driver), Cool, Heat, Auto as the ACs have them; **Temperature** Keep or a value (in Auto on thermostats with heat and cool setpoints, Keep keeps both); **Fan** Keep or a speed. On, as it was hides the temperature and the fan. An action saved with a mode alone opens with Keep. Keep needs no newer driver.
- **Saying it.** The run's result (`NO_LAST_MODE` in `problems`; the app: "1 AC was left off: its last mode isn't known yet"), History (`counts.no_last_mode`, which the history takes from a run's problems, whoever ran it: a user, a schedule or a link; "1 AC left off: no last mode known yet; set it once"), the scene's action in the app ("On, as it was") and Composer's Print Schedules and Scenes (`on, as it was`). Before a run, the AC action editor and the scene's card in the list name the ACs such a step would leave off now (`last_mode` null, or one DirectorLink cannot set): "Not seen on yet: Living room AC, Bedroom AC — turn each on once, or they stay off" (four languages), so they can be turned on once before Shabbat. A **scheduled** run that leaves an AC off this way alerts the admins who chose "A schedule has a problem", once a run, as a run a device refused does (ADR-047): the same sealed alert, with the scene's name and nothing else.
- **Commands.** "Turn on the AC in the living room" (*הדלק את המזגן בסלון*, *enciende el aire del salón*, *accendi il condizionatore del soggiorno*) turns an AC that is off on in its last mode when the controller knows it (`last_mode`, one of its `modes`), with `PATCH /v1/thermostats/{id}` `{"mode": …}` alone, the same request as a tap; "living room AC to 23" also sets 23° in it (with heat and cool setpoints, only when the last mode is heat or cool: which setpoint). When one AC's last mode is not known, or the driver is older, it asks which mode, as before; a mode said is that mode.
- **Going back to 1.9.0.** 1.9.0 refuses the unknown mode in `Scenes.cleanSet`: it leaves the step out as it loads the scenes (the scene stays and runs its other steps; the log says so) and drops it at its next save of any scene. So, as for refrigerator steps (ADR-049) and music steps (ADR-057), 1.10.0 keeps these steps apart under `directorlink_scene_steps_3`, which 1.9.0 neither reads nor rewrites, and marks the scenes record `climate_steps_kept`, which 1.9.0 drops when it saves; back on 1.10.0 a record without the mark gets the steps back in their places (and a step removed on 1.10.0 stays removed). Checked with the real 1.9.0 driver code from aa95f86: it loads, runs and saves such a scene without the step, and 1.10.0 puts it back. 1.9.0 leaves `directorlink_last_modes` alone; back on 1.10.0 the modes seen meanwhile are missed until they change again. A 1.9.0 app shows the step as "On"; its Edit screen shows Cool picked but keeps the step unless a choice is changed, and saving the scene sends it back unchanged. A 1.10.0 app with a 1.9.0 driver offers no On, as it was and asks the mode as before; Keep works.

**Why:** Each AC back as the family left it is what "turn the ACs on" means at home, and only the controller sees every way an AC is set, so it remembers. Nothing but the mode is sent because the AC keeps its own setpoint and fan while it is off; sending them would be choosing for the family. Never a guessed mode, because a wrong guess heats the house in summer or cools it on a winter Shabbat, when nobody may change it: an AC left off, and said so, is the safer mistake, and is set right once for good. A store of its own rather than with a thermostat's state, which Director gives again at every start and DirectorLink keeps only in memory.

**Consequence:** An AC never seen on since the update (one off since then, or added later) is left off by such a step until the family turns it on once. The controller writes its persistent data once each time a thermostat's last mode changes (a mode change other than to off). The cloud learns nothing new: scene steps, thermostats and History travel sealed as before (docs/ACCOUNTS.md: devices, states and commands, never); a schedule alert for a run that left an AC off is, to the cloud, the same as any other schedule alert (admin keys only, padded to one size). No D1 migration, nothing to deploy.

## ADR-069 — Control4's Relay Door, Gate and Garage Door Controllers are doors and gates, opened with their own Open

**Context:** DirectorLink opened a door or gate only through a KNX Contact/Relay device (ADR-036) and a DoorBird's button (ADR-050). A door or gate on any other relay (a DoorBird's, the DirectorLink · DoorBird driver's relay connection, a contact/relay module's) is in Control4 a **Relay Door, Gate or Garage Door Controller** (`door_relay_control.c4z`, `gate_relay_control.c4z`, `garagedoor_relay_control.c4z`, Snap One's): a driver with one uibutton proxy (its button in the Control4 app), its relays bound to its connections 1 (Open/Toggle), 2 (Close) and 3 (Stop), its optional Closed Contact (4) and Opened Contact (5), its variable `STATE` (`Opened`, `Closed`, `Partial`, `Unknown`) and events Opened (1), Closed (2), Partial (3), Unknown (4). On the owner's controller the gate at the DoorBird is such a controller (530, its proxy 531, in its own room), while the KNX doors stay KNX Contact/Relay devices. The owner decided that these controllers are doors and gates in DirectorLink, with everything a door has. Read from Snap One's drivers (versions 10 and 11, one code for all three): `ExecuteCommand("OPEN")` (programming's Open, the action Open Gate) opens. With Relay Configuration Pulse (the default) it pulses the Open/Toggle relay for its Open/Toggle Relay Pulse Time (500 ms by default, 50 ms to 60 s), releasing the Close and Stop relays first and the Open relay itself after; with one relay the pulse is the gate's own button (it may close an open gate) and is not sent while a contact says it is open. With Relay Configuration Hold, OPEN holds the Open relay until CLOSE or STOP; the Fail Safe time ends the hold only with three relays (it then sends Stop), so a held controller with one or two relays stays open until it is closed in Control4. SELECT (the Control4 app's button) closes whatever is not `Closed`. Without a contact `STATE` is only what the controller was last told (`Opened` at once after an OPEN with two or three relays, and a second OPEN fires no event, the state being unchanged; always `Unknown` with one pulsed relay). Another driver cannot read a driver's properties (ADR-049), but Director says what is bound to a device's connection (`C4:GetBoundProviderDevice`) and gives a driver's `<devicedata>` (`C4:GetDeviceData`, ADR-059, ADR-065).

**Decision (1.10.0):**
- **Recognized by their driver file** (`src/adapters/classifier.lua`, any case, and Composer's second download `… (1).c4z`, as ADR-056 does for Hikvision), from the proxy's protocol driver (`src/control4/normalize.lua`): each controller's button is a door (`kind` `door`, `gate` or `garage_door`) under the button's id, with its name, in its room (`src/adapters/relay_controller.lua`). A controller with nothing bound to its Open/Toggle relay opens nothing and is not listed (`/v1/devices` lists it, unsupported). Before the adapters start, each controller's connections are read (five `C4:GetBoundProviderDevice` calls a controller, at every project read; Director's OnPIP, which comes when bindings change, reads the project again); a Director that cannot say is taken as bound, without contacts: an error, and any answer that is not a number (nil, a table), whose meaning is not known; only a number says what is bound (0: nothing).
- **Opening is the controller's own Open, once** (`C4:SendToDevice(controller, "OPEN", {})`): one short press of its Open relay as the installer set it up (its pulse time, its Invert settings, its Close and Stop relays released first, its contacts' "already open"). DirectorLink never sends CLOSE, STOP, SELECT or anything to a relay of the controller's: a gate closing or stopping on someone is not the app's to do, and SELECT closes a gate whose state is not known. `scripts/check_package.py` holds that (`check_door_controllers_open_only`: the adapter's only command to a controller is its Open; never `"CLOSE"`, `"STOP"`, `"SELECT"`, `"TOGGLE"`, `"TRIGGER"`, `"LUA_ACTION"`). **Pulse only (ADR-036):** where Director's `<devicedata>` gives the controller's Relay Configuration as `Hold` (a `<value>`, never its `<default>`), Open would hold the door open: `409 HOLD_NOT_ALLOWED` and nothing sent unless Relay Hold is Allowed, and a scene's step is refused (failed, `HOLD_NOT_ALLOWED`) whatever it says. Where Director gives no value, the controller is taken as the default, Pulse, and the log says so once a set-up (`relay_configuration`: Pulse, Hold or "not given by Director"). The property is read again at each opening (one `C4:GetDeviceData`): Composer sends no project event when an installer sets Hold; where Director gives no value then, the last value read stands. `PATCH /v1/relays/{id}` on a controller's door: `409 NOT_SUPPORTED` (no relay of DirectorLink's to hold or release), after the usual checks.
- **Its state, only with a contact.** `door_state` (`open`, `closed`, `partly_open`) when the controller has a Closed or Opened Contact bound, from `STATE` at set-up and its events after; null otherwise, and for `Unknown`. `state` (the relay's contact) is null and `state_reported` false: the relay is the controller's.
- **Everything a door has, unchanged:** it is a relay to every part of DirectorLink (`device.kind` `relay`): `GET /v1/relays` (with `kind` and `door_state`, both new fields; a KNX relay's `kind` is `relay`), the two taps, Door Control, `Access.canOpen` (doors and the room, ADR-054, ADR-061), History, the doors-opened alert, ask-to-open links (ADR-058), scenes' door steps (pulse only, never by DirectorLink's own runs), favorites, commands by its name (a door to the parser), backups (by id, never moved by name), Turn off all leaving it alone.
- **Opened elsewhere** (in the Control4 app, a keypad, a remote): the controller going from `Closed` to `Opened` or `Partial` is an opening in Control4 (History `who` `control4`, and the alert), at most one a door a minute, as ADR-050's. Without a contact, with two or three relays, that is an OPEN sent in Control4 after a CLOSE set `Closed` (an OPEN after an OPEN fires nothing, and is not seen); with one pulsed relay and no contact nothing is seen. DirectorLink's own Open counts as its own until the controller says `Closed` again after `Opened` or `Partial` (then the next opening is someone else's, however soon), at most 90 s (`Alerts.CONTROLLER_OWN_SECONDS`, not 15: a gate with only an Opened Contact says Opened once fully open); a `Closed` after `Unknown` (a contact read late) does not end it. What a KNX relay a controller drives reports itself counts 15 s, as any relay's: it reports at once.
- **One gate, once.** A controller whose Open/Toggle relay is a **KNX Contact/Relay DirectorLink shows** is that relay's door: one door under the relay's id, name and room (as before: scene steps, favorites, ask links, History, members' rooms and the commands people say keep naming it), with the controller's kind and state, opened with the controller's Open; `PATCH` still holds and releases that KNX relay as before (Relay Hold). The controller's button is then no door of its own (`/v1/devices`: another device). Its relay's own events are still watched (an opening in Control4 with one relay and no contact), and either seeing an opening is one entry. Chosen over listing the controller and aliasing the relay's id to it, because every reference, a 1.9.0 app's too, keeps working without moving anything; and over keeping both, which shows one gate twice. A KNX relay on a controller's Close or Stop connection is part of that door, not a door of its own (`/v1/devices`: an unsupported relay): its Open would close or stop the gate. Chosen over showing it without Open, which is a row with nothing to do there and which a 1.9.0 app would still offer to open; unbound in Composer, it is a door again under its id, everything kept. Two controllers on one Open relay (a button for one gate in two rooms) are both shown, as in Control4, and are each other's partners (and the log warns). A **DoorBird doorbell** whose driver is the controller's relay (the official driver's relay connection) keeps its Open (its ring banner's), and the controller is a gate in its own room: both are shown, the doorbell as a doorbell. They are each other's partners: DirectorLink's command to one counts as its own for the other (no "opened in Control4" for the DoorBird's relay event after DirectorLink opened the gate, nor the other way), and one opening seen by both is one entry and one alert. A doorbell camera of the camera agreement (the DirectorLink · DoorBird driver) still opens nothing (ADR-065); a controller bound to its relay connection is the gate.
- **No feature flag:** `kind` and `door_state` are in every relay. A 1.9.0 app shows a controller's door as a door or gate ("Door or gate", Open with its two taps), and its pulse is the controller's Open. The 1.10.0 app shows "Gate · Closed" (its kind, and the state with a contact) in the door's row and favorite, in English, Hebrew, Spanish and Italian, a member without doors too (ADR-054: they see doors and their state), and explains `HOLD_NOT_ALLOWED` in its words; with a 1.9.0 driver (no `kind`) it shows "Door or gate" as before.
- **Part of the door, not another device (1.10.1):** in `/v1/devices` the button of a controller shown as its KNX relay, and a KNX relay on a Close or Stop connection, say `part_of`: the door's id (a DoorBird's button, intercom and camera: their doorbell's), only while that door is shown and to whoever sees it, and the app leaves a device that is part of one it shows (and an alarm partition Home shows) out of a room's "other devices", where a 1.10.0 app listed the gate's button as a device it cannot control; the API still lists it, and 1.9.0 and 1.10.0 apps ignore the field.

**Why:** The controller is how the installer made that door work in Control4: its Open does what the Control4 app's programming does, with the installer's pulse time, Invert settings and relays, so DirectorLink adds no knowledge of relays it cannot see; Open rather than SELECT because SELECT closes a gate whose state is not known, and never Close or Stop because the app cannot see the gate. The property read where Director gives it, because a Hold controller's Open is exactly the hold ADR-036 keeps behind Relay Hold. A state only with a contact, because without one the controller's state is only its last command. The KNX relay's id for one gate, because the owner's references (scene steps, favorites, ask links, History) and a 1.9.0 app name the relay; the partners, because one opening must be one entry and one alert.

**Consequence:** What the cloud learns: nothing new (docs/ACCOUNTS.md): a controller's door opened is the same sealed `door_opened` notice, to admins who chose it, and its questions the same `open_request`. What the controller does: five `C4:GetBoundProviderDevice` and one `C4:GetDeviceData` (its whole `<devicedata>`, about 30 KB) a controller at each project read, one `C4:GetDeviceVariables` and four event registrations a controller; one command per opening. Not yet seen on a real controller: what `C4:GetBoundProviderDevice` gives for a controller's dynamic relay connections and its contacts, whether Director's `<devicedata>` carries property values (if not, a Hold controller is opened as the default Pulse: its Open then holds as the installer set it, as it does from the Control4 app), and the controller's events reaching DirectorLink; the release's TESTING steps check them on the gate controller 530. A controller whose proxy still has its driver's name ("Relay Gate Controller (OS2.9+)") is listed by it: rename it in Composer. A binding changed in Composer moves a door between a controller's id and its KNX relay's (references to the other id wait: favorites show nothing, ask links open nothing, scene steps skip it; they work again if the binding comes back), which only an installer's change does. An ask-to-open link goes (`door_gone`) only once its device is no longer in the project, not while its door does not work (Director not saying what is bound for a read, a binding changed): in 1.9.0 any door not set up lost its links, and links are not in backups. Edges, by design: the partners are found through the controller's binding only (a DoorBird's host or MAC is in another driver's properties, ADR-049), so with gate 530 moved to the DirectorLink · DoorBird driver's relay the official DoorBird doorbell is no partner, and if its driver still reports the relay's trigger (event 104) each opening of the gate by DirectorLink is also an "opened in Control4" on the doorbell: keep 530 on the official driver's relay, or expect that entry. The gate and its DoorBird doorbell are two Opens for one gate: two quick Opens from either pulse the relay twice, which may stop or reverse a gate on one toggle relay, as two presses of its Control4 button do; not refused, because a second press is also how a gate that did not move is tried again, and without a contact DirectorLink cannot see it move. A door step made through the API for a room or the whole home (the app always names its doors) now opens the controllers' doors there too. Nothing new is stored. **DirectorLink 1.9.0** with this project: a controller's button is an unsupported device again; its favorites show nothing, its scene steps are skipped (not a door), and its ask-to-open links are removed at 1.9.0's first project read (`door_gone`; not in backups); a KNX relay shown as a controller's door is that relay again, everything kept. A 1.10.0 app with a 1.9.0 driver shows every relay as "Door or gate". No D1 migration; no Worker change.

## ADR-068 — Commands in Spanish and Italian: their own words, read in the app's language first, never another language's

**Context:** 1.10.0 shows the app in Spanish and Italian (ADR-067), and Say or type a command with it, its microphone listening in es-ES and it-IT; the parser (ADR-063, ADR-066) understood English and Hebrew only. The owner decided that commands understand Spanish and Italian in this release as fully as English and Hebrew, not as a trial. English and Hebrew never mix up (their letters differ), but Spanish, Italian and English share letters and some words with other meanings: *a* is "to" in Spanish and Italian and an article in English, *once* is eleven in Spanish, *in* + a number is a time in English and Italian, *e* is "and" in Italian while *è* is "is", *para* is "stop" and "for". One vocabulary for all of them would read a word of one language as another's.

**Decision (1.10.0):**
- **A language's words of its own** (`app/js/command-parser.js`, one module still, importing only `heaters.js`): each language is a lexicon (`lexicon()`) with its words for what to do, the kinds of devices (and which are the AC itself), modes, units, all and everything, don't, questions and times, the words for a change by a step and an amount, feelings, the words after which a feeling is a mode (*en*, *a*, *modo*; *su*, *in*, *modalità*), its numbers, its separators of two or three things, and the words before a number that make it a time. English and Hebrew stay one lexicon, word for word as in 1.9.0 and ADR-066.
- **Which words a sentence is read with.** `parseCommand(text, catalog, { language })` takes the app's language (`js/commands.js`). In Spanish and Italian it reads the sentence with that language's words first; when that reading understood nothing at all (no refusal either), with English and Hebrew; a refusal in either (*no*, *non*, a time, a feeling) stands; of two readings not understood, the one with fewer unknown words is said. In English and Hebrew (and with no language, as a 1.9.0 caller) only English and Hebrew. So English and Hebrew are understood in every language of the app (their command words are what many say anyway: *AC*, *play*, *stop*), Spanish only in Spanish and Italian only in Italian, and a Spanish or Italian sentence is never read with English words, nor the other way, nor Spanish as Italian. *Refinement considered:* every language in every app language; not chosen, because a word of one is a command of another (*once*, *a 7*), and the microphone listens in the app's language anyway.
- **The user's names are in any language.** The catalog has a room's Control4 name and every name of Settings → Rooms (four languages now, not only `en` and `he`). A word of a name that is a kind of device or a filler need not be said, in English and Hebrew and in the language the sentence is read in ("Luces del techo" by *techo*, "Tenda da sole" by *sole*; an English or Hebrew home's names are matched as in 1.9.0); *habitación*, *cuarto*, *camera* and *stanza* are "room" words, as *room* and *חדר* are.
- **Their grammar.** Articles and contractions are fillers (*el*, *la*, *los*, *las*, *del*, *al*; *il*, *lo*, *gli*, *le*, *della*, *nel*, *sul*), Italian elisions are two words (*l'aria*, *dell'ingresso*, *all'una*, *mezz'ora*); every imperative, familiar and polite, for one and for many, and the infinitive (*apaga*, *apague*, *apagad*, *apaguen*, *apagar*, the voseo *apagá*; *spegni*, *spenga*, *spegnete*, *spegnere*), with one or two pronouns on the verb (*apágalas*, *ciérrala*, *pónmelo*, *spegnile*, *chiudila*, *spegnerla*: only a verb, never a word of a name); accents optional, as everywhere (folded); number words (*veintitrés*, *treinta y cinco*, *dieciséis*, *ventitré*, *trentotto*, *y medio*, *e mezzo*, *a la mitad*, *a metà*), *por ciento*, *per cento* (and English *per cent*), *grados*, *gradi*, *°C*, and Spanish *A/A*. Spanish plurals of words that end in a consonant are another form of the name (*salón*, *salones*; *luz*, *luces*); a name said with the other ending of gender (*niñas* for *niños*, *bambini* for *bambine*), only that way, is asked, as the other Hebrew plural is.
- **What differs from English.** *Sube*/*baja* and *alza*/*abbassa* are a step brighter or dimmer for the lights (in English *raise the lights* is on), louder or quieter for the music, the setpoint up or down with *temperatura* said or the degrees (*baja el aire dos grados*: a temperature is said with *a*, *a 22*, and no AC is set to 2°), up or down for the blinds; with the AC alone (*sube el aire*, *alza il condizionatore*) not understood, as *turn up the AC*. With up or down, *un poco* and *un po'* are a step for the lights, the music or the temperature (*baja un poco la luz*), never for the blinds. *Más*/*menos* and *più*/*meno* make a step with *luz*, *brillo*, *volumen* and their Italian words, and with an adjective (*más alta*, *más oscura*, *più forte*, *più scura*); with a word for warm or cold (*más frío*, *più caldo*) the AC, only with the AC said or *haz*, *pon*, *fai*, *metti*, as Hebrew *יותר קר*. An amount by *un*, *en* (*sube la luz un 20%*, *en 2 grados*) and *del*, *di* (*alza la luce del 20%*). *Pon música* and *metti la musica* play. A state (*encendida*, *abiertas*, *accesa*, *chiuse*) says how it is: a question, done nothing with, so that a question typed without its *?* or its accent (*e accesa la luce*) never acts; *¿* is a question mark too, and Italian *è* asks while *e* is "and".
- **Every rule of ADR-063 and ADR-066 in each language,** each with its own tests: a time is never a level (*a las 7*, *a las siete*, *a la una*, *dentro de 5*, *en 5 minutos*, *por 10 minutos*, *hasta las 10*, *mañana*, *alle 7*, *alle sette*, *all'una*, *tra 5*, *fra cinque minuti*, *per un'ora*, *fino alle 10*, *domani*; a number after *en* is a time unless a unit follows, so *pon el aire en 23* is not understood: *a 23* or *en 23 grados*; a number word after such a word is a time too, in every language (*a las siete*, *alle sette*; English *at seven*, which 1.9.0 did not understand); Italian *ora* is the hour, *adesso* and *subito* now; *luego*, *después* and *dopo* are later, except right after *y* or *e*: then); *no*, *nunca*, *deja*, *non*, *mai*, *lascia* never act; a feeling never acts (*tengo frío*, *hace calor*, *ho freddo*, *fa caldo*; *frío*, *calor*, *freddo*, *caldo* are a mode only after *en*, *a*, *modo*, *su*, *in*, *modalità* with the AC said); a bare *más*, *menos*, *un poco*, *più*, *meno*, *un po'* stays a change by an amount, refused; never a guess (which one, part of a name, a short name one letter off, the other ending); two or three things parted by *y*, *e*, *ed*, *poi*, a comma (not inside a number: *treinta y cinco*, *ventidue e mezzo*), all or nothing; a door only as its first tap; Turn off all only after its confirm (*apaga todo*, *spegni tutto*, *apaga todas las luces*); a room's All off (*apaga la cocina*, *spegni tutto in cucina*).
- **Heaters** by their Spanish and Italian names too (`app/js/heaters.js`, whole words, the plurals listed): *calentador*, *termo*, *calefacción*, *calefactor*, *radiante* (*suelo radiante*), *toallero*, *calientatoallas*, *estufa*, *radiador*, *caldera*, *termoventilador*, *convector*, *termosifón*, *infrarrojo*, *calor*, *agua caliente*; *scaldabagno*, *scaldacqua*, *riscaldamento*, *radiante* (*pavimento radiante*), *scaldasalviette*, *stufa*, *stufetta*, *termosifone*, *radiatore*, *caldaia*, *termoconvettore*, *convettore*, *termoventilatore*, *infrarossi*, *termostufa*, *calore*, *acqua calda* (and *boiler*, already English). Never a colour: *Luz cálida*, *Blanco cálido*, *Luce calda*, *Bianco caldo* are lights.
- **What the review before release made exact** (1.10.0): *la del salón*, *el del dormitorio*, *las de la cocina* (an article right before *de* or *del*) and *quella*, *quelle del soggiorno* are "the one of" a room: the kind of device said in the part before (*enciende la luz de la cocina y apaga la del salón*: the Salón's lights; *apaga el aire del salón y apaga el del dormitorio*: the bedroom's AC), or a device named by its own words (*apaga la de pie*); with nothing before them, not understood, never the room's All off. A change by an amount needs its unit in every language: *alza il volume di 10*, *alza la luce di 20*, *sube el volumen un 10* are refused as *by 30* is in English, never a level of 10; after *sube*/*baja*, *alza*/*abbassa* a number with its unit is the step (*sube la luz de la cocina 20%*, as *baja el aire dos grados*), a level is said with *a*, *al*, *hasta el* (*sube la luz al 80%*), and a number alone after them (*sube el volumen 10*) is not understood. *Un*, *del*, *di* are never "by" right after a time: *a la una 20%* keeps its time (refused); *en un 25%* is by 25%. *Noche*, *noches*, *notte* (and *night*, *לילה*) are times (*apaga la luz por la noche*). A room word alone (*en la habitación*, *del cuarto*, *in camera*, *in stanza*, as *in the room* and *בחדר*) asks which room, never the whole home's Turn off all; *todas las habitaciones*, *tutte le stanze* are the whole home. A numbered room said with its room word (*la luz de la habitación 2*, *camera 2*) is that room, not a light numbered 2 elsewhere (*Foco 2* in the kitchen). *È* parts nothing (*spegni l'isola è chiudi…* is a question). **Hebrew states are questions too:** *האור בחדר הורים דלוק*, *שער חניה פתוח*, *התריסים סגורים*, *המזגן בסלון כבוי* without their *?* do nothing ("I can only do things"), for the same reason as *encendida* and *accesa*: dictation and typing drop the *?* (the integrator decided this for safety; the owner may revisit it). Not *סגור*, whose letters are also the imperative (*סגור את התריס* closes); the imperatives act as before (*תפתח את שער החניה*, *כבו את האור*).
- Nothing changes in the API, the driver, the cloud, the stores or the app's strings: the answers are the keys ADR-063 and ADR-066 have, in es.js and it.js since ADR-067.

**Why:** Each language's own words, because the same letters mean different things in each, and a command read with another language's word is a guess; the app's language first, because that is the language the user chose and the microphone hears; English and Hebrew after it, because they were understood in every app before and their letters or words do not collide with a Spanish or Italian reading that understood nothing; a refusal from either reading kept, because "don't" and a time must never be lost to another reading. A state as a question, because Spanish and Italian ask yes or no with the same words as a command, and dictation or typing often drops the *?* or the accent. *Sube la luz* as brighter, because that is what it says in Spanish and Italian; *sube el aire* not, because it is as unclear as *turn up the AC*.

**Consequence:** A 1.9.0 app understands no Spanish or Italian: in a Spanish or Italian profile it shows Spanish or Italian (it loads `i18n/es.js` or `it.js` from the site, ADR-067), but understands commands only in English and Hebrew, including the Spanish or Italian examples it shows. A 1.10.0 app has all of this with a 1.9.0 or 1.8.0 driver: nothing is new on the controller. Going back loses nothing (nothing is stored). An app in Hebrew or English does not understand Spanish or Italian sentences; an app in Spanish does not understand Italian ones. Not understood yet: *pon el aire en 23*, *sube el aire*, *luces encendidas* as a command, a step for blinds and fans, and everything ADR-066 lists. Privacy is unchanged (docs/ACCOUNTS.md): the words stay on the device; spoken, the browser's speech service hears them in the app's language, as before.

## ADR-067 — Appearance and language on a page of its own; a text size per device; Spanish and Italian, every string

**Context:** The language, the theme and the colours sat at the top of Settings, above the rows of its pages (1.5.0), and the app had English and Hebrew only. The owner asked for them on a page of their own, for a text size, and for Spanish and Italian as full languages.

**Decision (1.10.0):**
- **Settings → Appearance and language** (`#/settings/appearance`, `SETTINGS_PAGES` gains `appearance`): one row on Settings' list, between Account (Alerts since, below) and App, saying the language shown, the theme and the colours (and the text size when not the default), like the other rows. The page has Language (Auto says which language it found), Theme and colours, and Text size, and says what follows the user: the language, theme and colours are the profile's `language`, `theme` and `palette` (`js/profile.js` has saved and applied exactly these since 0.12.0; favorites and hidden rooms follow too, from their own screens); the text size does not. Before connecting it says the choices are kept on this device.
- **Settings → Alerts** (`#/settings/alerts`, `SETTINGS_PAGES` gains `alerts`; the owner's decision): "Alerts on this device" leaves Settings → Controller for a page of its own, its row between Account and Appearance and language. The row is for whoever may have alerts on this device, as the card was (a key, with DirectorLink 1.7.0 on the controller; only an admin's before), signed in or not, and says how they are: Off; On, with how many of the kinds this key may choose are on once the controller has said (`On · 4 of 7 kinds`); or what they still need, as the card's hint says it (Sign in to get alerts, Link this device to your account first, Add to Home Screen to get alerts, Needs iOS 16.4 or later, Notifications are blocked, This browser can't show alerts, or the account service out of reach). Signed out, the page says that alerts come through the account and offers to sign in, coming back to it. A member of a controller before 1.7.0 has no row, and the page, opened by its address, says that alerts are for admins there. A door's Ask screen links to the page. Who gets which alerts, and what is registered where, is unchanged; a 1.9.0 app keeps the card on Settings → Controller.
- **Text size: Small, Default, Large, Larger** (0.9375, 1, 1.125, 1.25 of the browser's default font size). Every `font-size` in `styles.css` is in `rem` and the root's is `calc(100% * var(--text-scale))`, set by `data-text-size` on `<html>`, so every screen scales; spacing, icons and the layout do not (a zoom would turn a 320 px phone into a 256 px one). What then did not fit was fixed for every size and language: the app's page titles wrap and push the header's actions to a line of their own rather than being cut (names from Control4, `dir="auto"`, keep their ellipsis), buttons wrap long labels instead of widening the page, camera names take two lines, the tab labels stop growing at 13 px so that five tabs fit 320 px, and the command field's hint stays at 15 px at most. `theme-boot.js` applies it before the first paint, as the palette and theme.
- **The text size stays on the device** (`localStorage` `directorlink.textSize`), not in the profile: one user has a phone and a big screen, which need different sizes, and a size that followed them would be wrong on one of them. It also needs nothing from the controller: a profile field would need a driver that knows it (a 1.9.0 driver refuses an unknown preference with `400`).
- **Default is the browser's default font size** (`100%`): 16 px in nearly every browser, as the app's pixel sizes were, and now also larger for someone who set a larger default in their browser.
- **Spanish and Italian** (`i18n/es.js`, `i18n/it.js`), every string of `en.js`, in the informal *tú* and *tu* that home apps use, with the same plural rules (`Intl.PluralRules`: one and other) and `Intl` numbers, dates and times in the language. The disclaimer word for word: "DirectorLink es un proyecto independiente, sin afiliación con Control4 ni Snap One." and "DirectorLink è un progetto indipendente, non affiliato a Control4 né a Snap One.". `scripts/check_app.py` now holds every language to `en.js`: exactly its keys, the same `{placeholders}` per string (a plural's forms together; the two command examples may give the room as `{inRoom}`, as Hebrew does), plurals where English has them with `other` (and `zero` where English has one), the disclaimer, `theme-boot.js` knowing the language and `sw.js` saving its file. The service worker saves every language at install: an update replaces the offline copy, and the app must open offline in the language it shows. Auto now picks Spanish or Italian from the browser's languages.
- **Say or type a command** shows in every language, with its own words translated; the microphone listens in the app's language (`speech` in `LANGUAGES`: en-US, he-IL, es-ES, it-IT). The parser understands Spanish and Italian sentences too, in the app's language (ADR-068).
- **Alerts** take the app's words in its language, as before (`js/alerts.js`), so Spanish and Italian alerts need nothing more. The Jewish calendar's months, holidays and readings are the Hebrew names as English spells them, with digits; Composer's names, the API console, the driver's documentation and the website stay in English. Room names get a field per language in Settings → Rooms (four now; the driver keeps up to ten a room).

**Why:** Settings' list had grown into rows; the choices at its top were the only exception. A text size through `rem` scales every screen from one value without touching each view, keeps the layout's widths for a 320 px phone, and stays pure CSS before the first paint. A language is only as good as its least-translated screen, so the check refuses a missing string rather than letting it fall back to English.

**Consequence:** Nothing on the controller changes: `language` was already any tag (`es` and `it` included), so a 1.9.0 driver keeps a Spanish or Italian profile, and going back keeps it. A 1.9.0 app still open on a device whose user chose Spanish elsewhere loads `i18n/es.js` from the site (where 1.10.0 is) and shows it, its own newer keys in English; offline, English; its Settings shows no Spanish choice and redraws once a minute as it applies the profile again. A 1.10.0 app with a 1.9.0 driver: everything here works (the text size is local). The cloud learns nothing new. The service worker's install saves two more files (280 KB, about 77 KB compressed).

## ADR-066 — Commands that do more: two or three things in one sentence, changes by a step, and heaters left as they are

**Context:** ADR-063 (1.9.0) understood one thing a sentence, and refused changes by a step ("warmer", "a bit brighter") with changes by an amount ("by 20%", "יותר"). People say two things at once ("kitchen lights off and close the blinds", "כבו את האור במטבח ותסגרו את התריסים", "turn off the living room lights and set the AC to 23") and steps ("warmer", "יותר אור", "תגביר את המוזיקה"). The owner's heaters are KNX lights named in Hebrew ("דוד הורים", "דוד ילדים", "חימום רצפה", "מחמם מגבות") that Composer programming keeps on or off; on the owner's controller a room's "lights off" turned off "דוד הורים" (1.9.0 left heaters out of "the lights" only to turn on or set a level), and Home's Turn off all and a room's All off turned them off as any light. Still without AI and on the device, with every rule of ADR-063: never a guess, a "?" never acts, a time is never a level, a feeling or "don't" never acts, a door only as its first tap, Turn off all only after its confirm, only what the user may control.

**Decision (1.10.0):**
- **Two or three things in one sentence** (`app/js/command-parser.js`). A sentence is parted at *and*, *then*, a comma or a semicolon, *ואז*, *וגם*, *ואת*, or a Hebrew word with ו in front of a verb or a word for a device ("ותסגרו", "והמזגן", "ומזגן"), only where something new starts (a verb, a kind of device, or a word of a device's or a scene's name, after "the", את or a room's name if any) and both sides say more than a verb or a room's name alone ("Kitchen, lights off" is one thing; "kitchen lights on and off" is one that contradicts itself, not understood). Never inside a name said ("Rock and Roll", "סרט ומוזיקה"), never at a word that is itself a command word or a word of a name ("וילון", a room "חדר ורד"), never before a room alone ("the lights in the kitchen and the living room" is still two rooms at once). At most three parts (more: "Up to 3 things at a time"). Each part is read as a sentence of ADR-063, and takes from the others what it does not say: the room said whole in another part (in a device's name too: "the kitchen island"), from the part before, or from the part after when the two share one verb ("turn off the lights and the AC in the living room"); and the verb of the part before (or after) when it has no verb, number or mode of its own ("…and the AC"), or none when the part is understood without it ("…and good night", a scene). **All or nothing:** a refusal in any part (don't, a time, a feeling) refuses the sentence; a part not understood, that would ask (its question and options are named, to say again) or that cannot be done makes the whole sentence that answer, with the part's words: "In “close the garage door”: I didn't understand “garage”. Nothing was done." A "?" anywhere is a question. Two parts that would change the same device are refused ("turn off the kitchen lights and turn on the island": "Two parts change Island"; a room's All off and its lights too), one door or gate a sentence ("One door or gate at a time"), and the whole home's Turn off all in a part that does not say *all* or *everything*, while another part names a room, asks which room (the home or that room would be a guess). Turn off all said in two parts is one, with one confirm ("turn off everything and close the blinds"). The parser answers `{ status: "ok", actions }`.
- **Each part shown, then done as a tap** (`app/js/commands.js`, `views/command.js`). Every part is shown with what it understood and Working…, then its own result. A door's or gate's part is its first tap (its own button in "Tap again to open" for 5 s), a scene that opens doors its Run button, and Turn off all its own confirm with the counts, Turn off and Cancel (10 s, then "Not done."); the other parts are done at once, not held back by them. *Refinement considered:* holding every part until the door's second tap; not chosen, because that second tap is the door's own button (which opens only the door) and the user asked for the other things as surely. Once every part is done or cancelled the answer goes after 8 s.
- **Changes by a step, only with their own words.** Lights: *brighter*, *brighten*, *dimmer*, *darker*, *more light*, *less light*, *increase*/*decrease* (with a light said), *a bit*, *a little*, *slightly*, *קצת*, *טיפה* with them or with *dim* ("dim the lights a bit"), *תגביר*/*תנמיך* (every form) *את האור*, *יותר אור*, *פחות אור*: 20 points from each light's level, or the amount said with its unit (*by 30%*, *ב-30%*, *30% brighter*), from 1 to 100; a light that is off goes to the step when brighter and stays off when dimmer (dimmer never turns one off); lights that only switch are left out, and a named one says so. The AC: *warmer*, *hotter*, *cooler*, *colder*, *יותר*/*פחות* *חם*/*קר* in either order, and in Hebrew *תעלה*/*תוריד* (every form) *את המזגן*: 1°, or the degrees said with their unit (*2 degrees warmer*, *by 2 degrees*, *ב-2 מעלות*, *בשתי מעלות*), at most 10°; each thermostat that is on, from the setpoint of its mode (heat or cool; with two setpoints in auto it asks which), kept within its range ("Living Room AC is already at its highest, 30°."); an AC that is off: "The AC in Bedroom is off. Turn it on first, or say the temperature." A comparative needs the AC said (*AC*, *temperature*, *thermostat*, *מזגן*…) or *make* (*תעשה*): otherwise it says how warm it is ("it's colder in the bedroom"), and with *I*, *I'm*, *me*, *feel* or *לי* how warm the user feels ("make it warmer for me", "יותר חם לי"): both are refused as feelings. English "turn up the AC" stays not understood (more cooling, or a higher setpoint?); Hebrew *תעלה את המזגן* is a higher setpoint, as it is said. Sonos: *louder*, *quieter*, *softer*, *turn the music up*/*down*, *volume up*/*down*, *raise*/*lower the volume*, *increase*/*decrease* and *תגביר*/*תנמיך* with the music or the volume said, *תעלה*/*תוריד את הווליום*: 10 (or *by N%*) on each Sonos room's own volume, from 0 to 100. Without these words, *more*, *less*, *a bit*, *by*, *יותר*, *פחות*, *קצת* and a number with ב stay a change by an amount, refused as in 1.9.0 ("kitchen lights more", "kitchen lights by 20%", "תדליק את האור ב-20%"); an amount needs its unit ("brighter by 30", "תגביר את האור ב-7", *alza il volume di 10* are refused, never a level); a step with a level ("brighter to 30%") or a mode is not understood. The parser says `brightnessBy`, `temperatureBy` or `volumeBy`; the app works out each device's value from what it shows when the command runs, and sends the PATCH a slider or a button sends.
- **Heaters are left as they are wherever "all the lights" is meant: one rule for commands and buttons** (`app/js/heaters.js`, the words of ADR-063, whole words in the singular or plural with their Hebrew prefixes, and "hot water", "hot tub", "מים חמים"). The parser, commands.js, Home's Turn off all (`turn-off.js`), a room's All off (`controls.js allOff`) and the room's screen import it; no other list. A command's "the lights" of a room or of the home (on, off, a level, brighter or dimmer) never include a heater unless it names it; Home's Turn off all and a room's All off, as buttons and as commands ("turn off all the lights", "turn off everything", "kitchen off"), leave heaters as they are: not sent and not counted ("Turn off all 6" while 7 lights are on, the seventh "דוד הורים"). Each says so: a command under what it understood, for the heaters it would have changed ("Kitchen: lights off" — "The heater “דוד הורים” is left as it is."; on for off, off for on); Turn off all with its second tap and its result ("Turn off all leaves the heater “דוד הורים” as it is."), or alone when only heaters are on and there is no button; a room's All off under its button while a heater is on, the button disabled when only heaters are on. A heater's own switch, and a command that names it ("turn off the towel warmer", "תכבו את דוד הורים"), still switch it. A room or a home whose only lights are heaters: "The only lights in X are heaters, left as they are. To switch one, say its name." Scenes and schedules are not changed (their steps are what an admin chose), nor `POST /v1/off` or the driver: they turn off exactly the devices the app names, and no driver code builds such a list itself.
- **What the review before release made exact** (1.10.0). A room's name inside the name of a device of another room ("רחצה ספוטים כניסה", a light of the parents' room, has the Entrance's name) is that device's, not the room said: never carried to another part ("…ואת המזגן" is not the Entrance's AC), and a part that would then be the whole home's Turn off all asks which room, as when another part names a room. A heater one part switches is never "left as it is" in another part's note ("כבו את האורות בחדר הורים ואת דוד הורים"). Dictation's periods part a sentence as a comma does ("Kitchen lights off. Close the blinds.": two things, all or nothing; one at the end parts nothing), and "a.m.", "p.m." are times. "Turn off the heating" ("תכבה את החימום", *apaga la calefacción*) without *all* asks which room: it may be the AC, floor heating or a heater wired as a light. A room and a device of another room named alike, said alone ("apaga el termo": a room "Termo" and the heater "Termo" in the bathroom), ask which. A word for "don't", a time or a feeling in a name ("Mañana", "Good night", "Lámpara de noche", "אור חם") counts as that name only when the whole name is said; otherwise it refuses the sentence, which then never lets the microphone's other guesses act. A scene named like a command ("All off", "Apaga todo") still runs as that scene when its name is the sentence (ADR-063), without Turn off all's confirm: it does what an admin made it do, as its Run button; "turn off everything" is still Turn off all with its confirm.
- Nothing changes in the API, the driver, the cloud or the stores.

**Why:** Two things at once, because that is how a family says it, and each part is still understood by the same rules, so nothing new is guessed; all or nothing, because a sentence done in part is a guess about the rest; the room said once, because people do not repeat it, and only whole, so a typo in one part is never carried into another. The other parts done beside a door's second tap, because the second tap is the door's own and opens nothing else. Steps only with their own words, because "more", "by 20%" and a number with ב as often say an amount, a time or a level, and a time must never be a level; an amount only with its unit for the same reason. Warmer with the AC said, because "it's warmer in the bedroom" says how it is and "חם לי" how one feels, which ADR-063 never acts on. Heaters left by every "all the lights", the buttons included, because they are kept on and off by Composer programming: turning one off with the room's other lights breaks that programming without anyone noticing (it did, on the owner's controller); naming one, or its own switch, is a clear wish. One rule in one module, so a command and a button never disagree about what a heater is.

**Consequence:** A 1.9.0 app against a 1.10.0 driver behaves as 1.9.0 (its Turn off all, All off and "lights off" still turn heaters off, and it understands none of the new sentences); a 1.10.0 app against a 1.9.0 or 1.8.0 driver has all of this, since nothing is new on the controller. Going back to 1.9.0 loses nothing (nothing is stored). A light that only sounds like a heater ("Heater lamp") is left out of All off and Turn off all too (naming it switches it); a mark per light in Settings is still open. "Turn off everything" at night no longer turns heaters off: Composer's programming does. Not understood yet: more than three things, one kind in two rooms at once, steps for blinds and fans, a step to a level, English "turn up the AC". Privacy is unchanged (docs/ACCOUNTS.md): the words stay on the device; the controller sees the same requests as taps (a step arrives as the value it reaches), the cloud nothing new.

## ADR-065 — Every DirectorLink camera driver on one agreement, recognized by a marker; a doorbell camera rings

**Context:** ADR-056 (1.8.0) passes on the alerts of one driver, the DirectorLink · Hikvision Camera driver, known by its file name (`DirectorLink-Hikvision-Camera.c4z`), its event 1 and its variable 1013. Every other camera driver of DirectorLink Drivers would need code of its own in DirectorLink, and the next one, **DirectorLink · UniFi Protect** (built by another agent in its own repository, against this decision), has doorbells too, whose rings DirectorLink knew only from the DoorBird's driver (its doorstation proxy and event 102, ADR-050). The owner decided one agreement, version 1, which no name of may change. What Director gives one driver of another: its variables with their names and values (`C4:GetDeviceVariables`), and its events only by id (`C4:RegisterDeviceEvent`, whose documentation says "knowledge of device event ID values are required"); `C4:GetDeviceData(id, tag)` gives the tags of its `<devicedata>`, `<events>` among them ("only the first two levels of XML", says the documentation; with no tag, all of it). Director tells no driver that another added a variable, other than through the `OnVariableAdded` system event, which fires for every variable of every driver of the project and whose payload is not documented.

**Decision (1.10.0):**
- **The agreement, version 1** (public, for driver authors: docs/CAMERA_DRIVERS.md). The driver behind a camera proxy sets the string variable `DIRECTORLINK_CAMERA` to `"1"` (the version) and `DIRECTORLINK_CAMERA_KIND` to `"camera"` or `"doorbell"`. A detection worth alerting: it sets the string variable `LAST_ALERT` to a label, then fires the event named exactly `Alert`. A doorbell press: it sets `LAST_RING` (ISO 8601, UTC, a new value for every ring), then fires the event named exactly `Ring`. Optional and recommended: the string variable `DIRECTORLINK_CAMERA_EVENTS`, `"Alert=<id>,Ring=<id>"` (a camera: `"Alert=<id>"`), the ids its `driver.xml` gives those events (names without case or spaces around, whole ids from 1, comma separated; a name the agreement does not know is left for a later version). Pictures and live video go through Control4's camera proxy as before (ADR-055 reads the address, the login and the snapshot path from the proxy). One camera a driver. The version is a whole number from 1; a higher one is read as version 1 (a later version only adds); anything else is no marker. A kind other than `doorbell` (missing too) is a camera.
- **Recognized by the marker** (`src/control4/camera_drivers.lua`, `src/adapters/camera.lua`). When a camera is set up (a project read, Refresh Project, its driver updated), DirectorLink reads its driver's variables by name: the marker, the kind, `DIRECTORLINK_CAMERA_EVENTS`, and the ids of `LAST_ALERT` and `LAST_RING` (one that is not there yet is looked for again by name at the event). The events: the ids `DIRECTORLINK_CAMERA_EVENTS` gives when it names what the kind needs (`Alert`, and a doorbell's `Ring`), with no `C4:GetDeviceData` at all; a value set otherwise is not used (logged, warn, with what it said and why) and the events are looked for by name, as when it is not set: in `C4:GetDeviceData(driver, "events")`, else in the whole `<devicedata>`, as escaped text too, names without case or spaces around, comments left out, CDATA read as text, the first event of a name in the text. Each found is watched (`C4:RegisterDeviceEvent`) and routed to the camera by the manager. The log says at Info, once each time a camera is set up, how its events were found (`events_by`: `by DIRECTORLINK_CAMERA_EVENTS`, `by name from Director`, `Hikvision event 1` or `not found`), and at Debug what `C4:GetDeviceData` gave (its shape: how long, how many `<event>` tags, the names found; never the text), so a home's Director log shows whether Director names the events. A driver with several camera proxies is not watched (logged, warn): one event could not tell its cameras apart. A marked driver without an `Alert` (or a doorbell without a `Ring`) is not watched for it (logged, warn, naming the event missing and how many events Director named).
- **The marker may come later.** Each minute the scheduler's tick has the camera adapter look at the next five cameras (`Camera.LOOK_PER_TICK`, one `C4:GetDeviceVariables` each, all cameras in turn), and a camera whose marker, kind or `DIRECTORLINK_CAMERA_EVENTS` is not what it was set up with is set up again as at a driver update (`Manager.lookAgain` → `setUpAgain`, ADR-059), logged. What adds is taken at once; what takes away (the marker gone, a doorbell back to a camera, the events variable gone) only when the next look at that camera says the same, so one read of a driver Composer is reloading (before its `OnDriverInit` adds its variables again) drops no doorbell. A driver updated in Composer (a new `<version>`) is read again by ADR-059's watch, events and all. A camera whose drivers Director gives no variables for is left as it is.
- **The Hikvision drivers.** A driver with the marker is read by the marker only, whatever its file name; the DirectorLink · Hikvision Camera driver (by its file name, any case, also `… (1).c4z`) is still taken without it, as in 1.8.0 (ADR-056): its event 1, and `LAST_ALERT` by name, else 1013 (its `driver.xml` is not read). With the marker and no events named (by its `DIRECTORLINK_CAMERA_EVENTS` or by Director), the Hikvision driver's `Alert` is still event 1. One driver is one camera either way: DirectorLink registers each pair of driver and event once a run, so the same Alert is never heard twice, and a camera's alert is sent once.
- **Labels.** `LAST_ALERT` is read without case, spaces, `_` and `-` (all left out before it is compared, so `LicensePlate` is `License Plate`): ADR-056's labels, and new ones `Animal` (`animal`), `Package` (`package`) and `License Plate` (`license_plate`; also `Licence Plate`). The app and its service worker say them in the app's language, English, Hebrew, Spanish or Italian (Animal, Package, License plate; בעל חיים, חבילה, לוחית רישוי; ADR-067); an app whose worker does not know a label says "Alert".
- **A doorbell camera is a doorbell.** A camera whose driver says `doorbell` is also a doorbell, under the camera's own id (the camera's `device.doorbell`, sharing its state; `Registry.doorbellList`, `Registry.getDoorbell`): `GET /v1/doorbells` lists it, its `camera` is itself, `can_open` false (`POST …/open` answers `409 NOT_SUPPORTED`, before Door Control and the caller's doors are looked at: no setting would let it open; a gate at it is its own Control4 device, which DirectorLink shows only as a KNX Contact/Relay, docs/CAMERA_DRIVERS.md), `connected` and the DoorBird's other times null; `GET /v1/devices?type=doorbell` lists it as a doorbell (elsewhere in `/v1/devices` it is a camera, once). Its `Ring` is a ring as a DoorBird's 102: `last_ring_at` (the driver's `LAST_RING` when it is new, later than the ring before, and from two minutes behind the controller's clock to five seconds ahead of it; else the moment the event came, so a `Ring` without a new `LAST_RING` is a new ring; never at or before the ring before, a second after it then), its last 20 rings in `events` (the app's room shows 5; kept through a refresh and a set-up again; after a restart its last ring is `LAST_RING`, unless that is ahead of the controller's clock; an app counts a ring only when `last_ring_at` is later than the one it knows, so a restart that gives an older time shows no banner), Home's banner with that camera's live picture (the app, unchanged, shows a doorbell's camera so, and keeps the camera's tile out of the room it is shown in), and the sealed `doorbell` alert, brief, at most one in 30 s, to whoever sees that doorbell (`Access.canSee` of a doorbell: every user in its room; its picture only with cameras, ADR-054). Rings are not in the history, as a DoorBird's are not. It stays a camera: in Cameras, its pictures, its `Alert` a camera alert. `inventory` counts it among the cameras and the doorbells. Doors opened is not offered for it (it opens nothing). A favorite `doorbell:<id>` of it comes back with a restore.
- **No API change** but descriptions (api/openapi.yaml: Doorbells, `Doorbell.camera`, `connected`, `last_ring_at`, `inventory.doorbells`, the camera alert choice and `features.camera_alerts`, `/v1/devices`' `type`, the open's 409). No new store, no feature flag: a doorbell camera is a doorbell in `/v1/doorbells`, which every app reads.
- **Tests and tools.** `driver/tests/test_camera_drivers.lua` (fake drivers of the agreement in `Mock.withAgreementCameras`: alerts by name, rings, the marker set late, a kind that changes, a driver update, events only in the whole `<devicedata>` or nowhere, two cameras on one driver, Hikvision drivers with and without the marker, `DIRECTORLINK_CAMERA_EVENTS` set, set wrong and set late, the log of how events were found, ring times, one empty read of a driver's variables, opening, `/v1/devices?type=doorbell`); tests/app/doorbell-push.test.mjs (a `last_ring_at` that goes back is no ring); `scripts/dev_server.py --agreement-cameras` (a camera, driver 157, and a doorbell, driver 158; `alert 157 Animal`, `ring 158`); `scripts/check_contract.py` checks a doorbell camera against the contract.

**Why:** A marker the driver sets rather than a file name, because any driver of DirectorLink Drivers (and anyone's) then works without new code here, a renamed copy still works, and the installer sees it in Composer's variables. By name, because every driver numbers its own variables and events; the events by name in `driver.xml`, because Director lets one driver register another's events by id only, and the driver's `<devicedata>` is the documented way to read them (which is why the agreement asks for them in `driver.xml`, not `C4:AddEvent`). A few cameras a minute rather than `OnVariableAdded`, because that event would wake DirectorLink for every variable of the project at every start, with an undocumented payload, and a look at five cameras costs five Director calls a minute; a driver that learns its kind once it reaches the camera (a UniFi console says which cameras are doorbells) is then taken within minutes. The doorbell as the camera itself, because a doorbell camera is one proxy in Control4, the app already shows a doorbell with its camera, and the ring then reaches exactly whoever sees the doorbell, as a DoorBird's. One camera a driver, because the agreement's events carry no camera. `DIRECTORLINK_CAMERA_EVENTS`, because whether `C4:GetDeviceData` gives another driver's `<events>` is not documented clearly and not yet seen on a real controller, and the owner's controller cannot show it (its agreement drivers are Hikvision ones, whose event 1 is taken either way): a driver that says its ids itself needs nothing from Director but its variables, which every part of the agreement already reads; optional, so a driver without it still works where Director names its events. A ring's time only from a new `LAST_RING` not ahead of the clock, because a time ahead would make the banner last longer than a ring, and a `LAST_RING` reused for a second ring would make that ring look like the one before (no banner, a silent notification). The Hikvision drivers by file name until they set the marker, because the owner's eleven cameras run 1.0.6, which does not, and must not lose their alerts.

**Consequence:** What the cloud learns: nothing new (docs/ACCOUNTS.md). A doorbell camera's ring is the same sealed `doorbell` notice as a DoorBird's (brief, for whoever sees it) and its detections the same `camera` notice; more cameras may send camera alerts, within the same limits (one a camera a minute, 30 an hour). What the controller does: one `C4:GetDeviceVariables` per camera at set-up and five a minute after, one or two `C4:GetDeviceData` per camera of the agreement at set-up (the whole `<devicedata>` only when the `events` tag names none; none for a driver whose `DIRECTORLINK_CAMERA_EVENTS` says its ids). A marker that comes after DirectorLink started is taken within a minute or two in a home of ten cameras (about one minute every five cameras), a kind that changes as soon; until then a doorbell camera is a camera and its rings are missed. A driver whose events Director does not name in its `<devicedata>` is not watched (the log says so); not yet seen on a real controller: what `C4:GetDeviceData(id, "events")` and `C4:GetDeviceData(id)` give (the log's `events_by` and, at Debug, what Director gave say it on any controller), and a UniFi driver's marker added late (the release's TESTING steps check both); a driver that sets `DIRECTORLINK_CAMERA_EVENTS` does not depend on the first. **DirectorLink 1.9.0 with an agreement driver:** only the Hikvision camera driver, by its file name (its event 1 and 1013), whether it sets the marker or not (`DIRECTORLINK_CAMERA_EVENTS` is not read); any other driver's cameras show pictures only, with no alerts, and a doorbell camera is a camera (no ring, no banner, no ring alert). A 1.9.0 app with a 1.10.0 driver shows a doorbell camera as a doorbell (banner, rings, open hidden: `can_open` false) and says the new labels as "Alert". Going back to 1.9.0 loses nothing: nothing new is stored; a favorite `doorbell:<id>` of a doorbell camera stays in its profile and shows nothing there (1.9.0 lists no such doorbell, and does not mark it removed: the camera is there), and shows again after updating. A 1.10.0 backup restored by 1.9.0 drops such a favorite (1.9.0 finds no doorbell of that id). The cloud and the Worker are unchanged; no D1 migration.

## ADR-064 — The owner hands the home to another admin: the controller decides, the account service follows on its word

**Context:** ADR-054 made the home's owner the user who last claimed the home for an account (else the oldest admin): only they change their own access and devices and claim the home again, and (ADR-061) only they confirm bringing the owner's devices together. Handing the home to someone else was left open: a claim moves the home to the claiming account and removes every other account, and Composer's Revoke All API Keys starts over. The account service keeps an owner of its own, an account (`homes.owner_id`, the account that claimed), which decides more than its name says: only it approves accounts that join with an invitation made for another email (ADR-041), sees the home's accounts with their emails and removes them, cannot leave, replaces the home's relay secret (docs/RELAY.md), registers invitations for drivers before 1.0.0 and sees the backups when the driver names no admin (before 1.6.0); deleting it deletes the home with its members, invitations, alerts and backups (Apple's account-deleted keeps an account that owns a home); its homes' backups share 25 MB (ADR-048); a claim by that same account is no change of hands; the daily totals count a home while its owner's account exists (ADR-052). Sign out everywhere is how an owner ends a stolen session that could replace the secret. The two owners, the controller's user and the account service's account, met only at the claim.

**Decision (1.9.0):**
- **Only the owner, only to another admin.** In Settings → Users the owner, and only the owner, has **Make Dana the owner** on another admin user's row (`POST /v1/users/owner` `{"profile_id"}`, route role admin; `Access.mayMakeOwner`), asked first with what changes. Anyone else: `403 OWNER_ONLY`; while the owner cannot be known (a store unread at start): `503 UNAVAILABLE`; a member: `409 NOT_AN_ADMIN` (make them an admin first); the owner themself: `409 ALREADY_OWNER`. The controller records the new owner where it records the claimer, the people's store's `owner` (`People.setOwner`, which leaves the owner as it was when it cannot write), so every rule of ADR-054 and ADR-061 follows them at once: only they change their own access and devices, claim the home again, confirm bringing the owner's devices together and make someone else the owner. The old owner is an admin like any other: the new owner may change their access or remove their devices, and a suggestion to bring their devices together with another user's (ADR-061) stays a suggestion, which any admin who may change both users (the new owner, for one with theirs) confirms; nothing is merged by itself. Nobody is removed, at home or in the account service; no device, invitation, link, alert choice or permission changes. History: `access` `owner_changed` (`what` the new owner, `from` the old one, `who` the owner's key); logged.
- **Both agree, and neither alone moves it.** A home the account service never knew (its relay never accepted this home's identity) changes owner on the controller only: there is nothing to keep in step. Otherwise Remote Access must be on (`409 REMOTE_ACCESS_OFF`), and the controller asks the account service first, over its own relay connection, which only the home's secret opens: `{"type":"owner","id","account":"<tag>"}` (docs/RELAY.md), the new owner's Google or Apple account as the opaque tag of ADR-061 (`Users.ownerAccount`: the one account their devices that have exactly one all use; `null` when none), never a user's id or name. It is never guessed: when those devices use several accounts, `409 OWNER_ACCOUNT_UNCLEAR`, and when the account is also used by a device of the owner's own user (the owner once signed in on the new owner's phone), `409 OWNER_ACCOUNT_SHARED`, both naming the devices (`device_names`), and nothing is asked; the owner's app names the account's email in the confirmation, from the accounts it sees. The account service moves `homes.owner_id` to the account of the home with that tag, only when it belongs to the home and uses one of the admin keys the controller last listed, and answers `{"type":"owner_result","id","ok":true,"previous":"<tag>","moved":true}` (`moved` false when that account owned the home already: a retry), or `ok: false` with `NOT_CLAIMED` (no account owns the home there), `OWNER_NEEDS_ACCOUNT`, `ACCOUNT_NOT_ADMIN`, `INVALID_REQUEST` or `INTERNAL` (the controller then answers `502` with that code, `409` for `OWNER_NEEDS_ACCOUNT`). The controller records the new owner only after `ok`, or `NOT_CLAIMED` (then it moves alone), and only if Access still allows it (other requests ran meanwhile: the new owner made a member, the owner's key revoked); when it does not follow (Access says no, or the store cannot be written: `500`), nothing moves here and it tells the account service to undo that move: `{"type":"owner_cancel","id"}` with the request's id, on which the account service restores the owner account that request replaced (kept by request for 15 minutes in the home's object), while that request's account still owns the home and the old one is still a member, without asking for an admin key. No answer within 10 s: `503 REMOTE_TIMEOUT` ("may not have finished: try again"), nothing moves here, and the controller sends `owner_cancel` for that request, at once or, when the connection dropped, right after its next `hello` before anything else, so that a late or lost answer leaves the account service as the controller; trying again then moves it again, and the controller follows. Not connected when asked: `503 REMOTE_OFFLINE`, nothing is asked. The app waits 15 s for the controller's answer, and says every refusal in its own words (English and Hebrew). A new owner without an account of the home: `409 OWNER_NEEDS_ACCOUNT`, "Invite Dana's account first, and have them accept it": a tag exists only for an account that belongs to the home, which joins by an invitation. No session, app or route moves the account service's owner: only a claim, as before, and the controller's `owner`. Nothing the account service says makes anyone the owner on the controller: it can refuse, and `owner_result` counts only as the answer to a question the controller asked.
- **A new owner without an account: refused.** When an account owns the home and the new owner's devices use none at it (or one that does not belong to the home), the controller refuses (`409 OWNER_NEEDS_ACCOUNT`: "Invite Dana's Google or Apple account first, and have them accept it on one of their devices") rather than move alone: the old owner's account would go on approving joins, seeing the accounts and replacing the secret, and deleting it would delete the home, while the new owner could do none of it. The simplest rule that keeps one owner in both records.
- **What moves in the account service:** only `homes.owner_id`, and with it everything above: approving joins with another email (the requests waiting stay, for the new owner), the list of accounts and removing them, leaving (the new owner cannot, the old one can), replacing the secret, the registrations and backups of old drivers, the account whose deletion deletes the home and which Apple's account-deleted keeps, the 25 MB of that account's homes' backups, a claim by that account being no change of hands, and whose Sign out everywhere protects the secret. `claimed_at` stays the claim's. No D1 migration: one `UPDATE` of a column there is.
- **The app.** Settings → Users: **Make Dana the owner** on another admin's row, for the owner only, and a line on the owner's own row; the confirmation says what changes ("From now on only Dana changes their own access and devices, links the home to an account again, approves accounts that join with another email and replaces the home's secret, and their Google or Apple account, dana@example.com, becomes the home's account. You stay an admin, which Dana can change. Nobody is removed."; the account's email when the owner's app sees the home's accounts; nothing about an account in a home the account service never knew, which `GET /v1/users` says with `linked`); every refusal in its words. English and Hebrew.

**Why:** The controller decides who may do what (ADR-029), so it decides who the owner is among its users, and nothing the account service says may raise anyone. The account service's owner is an account, which it alone knows; it follows the controller's word over the connection only the home holds, as invitations are registered (ADR-032), so no session, a stolen one included, and no admin but the owner can move it. The account service first and the controller after, because the controller can wait for an answer and undo it (it holds the request), while the account service cannot ask the controller; undone by the request's id, because the account service then restores exactly what that request replaced, whoever it was, rather than a move of its own that would need the old account to pass the checks again. The account never guessed, because the account service gives its owner account every power over the home's accounts: the old owner's own account, or a kid's signed in on the new owner's tablet, must not become it. The tag, because the controller knows accounts only by tag (ADR-061), and the account service can tell whose it is. A claim was never the way: it removes everyone else.

**Consequence:** What the account service learns: that the home's owner changed and to which of its accounts (it knew which accounts belong), never which user or anything else of the controller's; and that the controller listed that account's keys as admin keys, which it knew. What the controller learns: whether an account owns the home in the account service, and whether the new owner's account belongs to the home. Someone holding the home's secret (a copy of the controller's storage, docs/ACCOUNTS.md) could also send `owner` and give the account service's owner to another account of the home that uses an admin key; with that copy they could already act as the owner's devices. An answer lost on the way leaves the account service moved and the controller not only until the controller's `owner_cancel` arrives (at its next connection, if the connection dropped; it is kept in memory, so a restart of the driver meanwhile loses it, and the owner then hands the home over again, which the account service answers as already done). Someone who could drop the controller's frames could keep the cancel from arriving; they could already keep any hand-over from finishing. A backup made before the hand-over, restored with its keys, brings back its owner on the controller (ADR-054), as with a claim made since the backup, while the account service keeps the new one: the owner then hands the home over again, which the account service answers as already done. A 1.8.0 Worker ignores `owner`: the transfer is refused after 10 s and nothing moves (deploy the Worker first, or with the driver). DirectorLink 1.8.0 after a downgrade reads the people's store's `owner` as its claimer: the new owner stays the owner, with all of 1.8.0's rules (only they claim); it cannot hand the home over again. A 1.8.0 app with a 1.9.0 driver shows the new owner's badge in People and devices (the old owner as an admin, with Edit) and no Make the owner; History shows the entry as one it does not know; its Accounts list is the new owner's. A 1.9.0 app with a 1.8.0 driver shows 1.8.0's People and devices, without Make the owner. Not yet seen on a real controller.

## ADR-063 — Say or type a command, without AI: understood on the device, by the user's own names

**Context:** The 1.9 roadmap asks for a field that understands simple sentences in English and Hebrew ("kitchen lights off", "living room AC to 23", "run Good night") by the user's own room, device and scene names, on the phone, without AI, within the user's rooms and permissions. The owner uses the app mostly as an iPhone Home Screen app. Browsers turn speech into text with the Web Speech API (`SpeechRecognition`, `webkitSpeechRecognition` in Chrome, Edge and Safari; not in Firefox), each with its own service; WebKit lists it in Home Screen web apps but does not listen there, while the keyboard's own dictation types into any field. The app's CSP runs only its own scripts.

**Decision (1.9.0):**
- **Where.** A field under Home's header (after the ring, offline and update banners), for everyone who controls something (not a 1.7.0 viewer key). From the other screens a button in the header opens the same field in a dialog, on screens 600 px wide and more (on a phone the header has no room for it beside the connection chip, and Home is one tap away); the `/` key opens it on any screen. English and Hebrew, right to left; a visible label for screen readers, Enter sends, Escape clears, the options and second taps are buttons, and what it understood and did is read out through the app's live region (in the dialog, which makes the rest of the page inert, through its own). The field itself is made once and kept: Home's redraws (every device change) and the dialog's change what is around it and never take it out of the page, so the keyboard's composition, iOS dictation and the caret are not cut (app.js leaves an element that stays the same in place, `replaceKeeping`). It takes 201 letters at most; more than 200 is not a command (so a longer paste cut short by the field is never understood in part).
- **Understood on the device by a small parser** (`app/js/command-parser.js`: no imports, no libraries, a few milliseconds a sentence in a home of 111 lights). Its words: verbs for what to do in English and in the Hebrew forms people say and type (imperative, future, infinitive; one or many: כבה, תכבה, כבו, לכבות), kinds of devices in the singular and plural, modes, percent and degrees, numbers in digits (23.5, 23,5) and words up to a hundred (twenty-three, עשרים ושלוש, שלוש עשרה, half, וחצי; a word from one to nine only with its unit, "five percent", so that "one of the lights" is not a light at 1%). It folds case, accents, niqqud and Hebrew final letters; takes up to two Hebrew prefixes off a word said (ו, ה, ב, ל, מ, ש, כ; a command word under them only from three letters, so "בבוקר" is not "קר"), but never off a name's own words other than their article ("חדר הילדים" said "חדר ילדים"; "בני" is not "שני"); takes plural and feminine endings off (אורות, מנורה, lights), but the two Hebrew plurals are not one form (בנים, boys, is not בנות, girls); takes a name's Hebrew word of four letters or more spelled with one vowel letter more or less, or doubled (חניה, חנייה; כניסה, כנסה; not the first or last letter); allows small typos in names (one letter in a word of four or more, two from eight; none in shorter words) and one letter in a command word of six or more ("night" is not "light", "deactivate" not "activate"); and takes names in any order. A name said only with a typo in a word under six letters, the other Hebrew plural, or a vowel letter more or less against three letters (גנה for גינה, as דנה for דינה) is asked ("Did you mean"), never done: in a short word one letter is often another name (Dana, Dina). A number word that is also in one of the names stays a word (שני). "Mode" and מצב are fillers ("cool mode", "במצב קירור"), סצנת is "scene", כל is "all" under a prefix too ("בכל הבית"), and °C, C, º, ℃, celsius and צלזיוס are degrees.
- **By the user's own names, from what the app already has for them**: rooms (the Control4 name and the English and Hebrew names of Settings → Rooms), and what the controller lists for this user (ADR-054): lights, thermostats, blinds and fans they control, their Sonos rooms (in the room each is shown in), the scenes they may run, and the doors and gates in their rooms (opened only with door access). In a device's or room's name, its kind and fillers need not be said ("Porch light" by "porch", "חדר ילדים" by "ילדים"); a device named only by its kind ("Light", "מזגן") is that device only with its room said; "the kitchen light" is a light of that name, "the kitchen lights" the room's.
