- **What it does** (each with its words in the app's README): lights on, off and to a level (a room's, or a named light); "the lights in a room" on or to a level never switch on a light named for heating (heaters, boilers, heat lamps and floor heating wired as lights: heater, heat, boiler, geyser, immersion, underfloor, towel, infrared, radiator, sauna, hot water, חימום, חום, תנור, דוד, בוילר, הסקה, מקרן, מחמם, מים חמים…, each a whole word in the singular or plural: "Warm white", "אור חם" and "חומה" are lights), only when it is named, and a user's only light, when it is such a heater, is not "the light"; AC off, to a mode, to a temperature, or both, within the thermostat's own range (an AC that is off asks which mode; one with heat and cool setpoints in auto, which setpoint; a change its setpoints cannot take says why); in a room with an AC and floor heating, "AC" (מזגן) is the room's thermostats that cool and a mode those that have it ("cool the living room" is its AC), "temperature" or "thermostat" all of them; blinds open, closed, to a position (only those that stop between) and stop; fans on and off; music play, pause, next and volume in a room (play, pause and next once a group); a scene, by its name alone or with run; a room's All off ("kitchen off", as on the room's screen); the whole home's Turn off all ("turn off everything": lights and AC; "turn off all the lights", "all the AC", "close all blinds"); and a door or gate opened.
- **Never a guess.** Every reading of the sentence (a device or scene, a room, both or neither) that uses every word is scored: the names' words matched (1 the same, 0.95 another form, 0.9 with a prefix, 0.75 a typo), a whole name more than part of one, and the plural (or "all", "כל") favours a room's devices, the singular a device of that name. Readings within 0.05 of the best that do different things ask "Which one?", each an option with its room (at most six; more: "say the room too"); part of a name ("island" for Kitchen Island) asks "Did you mean", even for one match; words it does not know answer "I didn't understand “…”" with three examples made from the user's own rooms and scenes (tapping one puts it in the field); a contradiction (on and off), two rooms or two kinds at once, a level out of range, a light that does not dim or a blind that does not stop between say so. A sentence that says not to ("don't", אל, לא), asks (is, are, what, האם, מה, or any "?": Hebrew asks yes or no with a command's own words, "האור במטבח כבוי?", and dictation writes "?" for a rising voice: "I can only do things, not answer questions"; since 1.10.0 a Hebrew state, דלוק, כבוי, פתוח, סגורים, is a question without its "?" too, ADR-068), names a time (at 7 pm, in 10 minutes, בבוקר) or a change by an amount (by 20%, more, יותר), or says how warm the user feels ("I'm cold", "חם לי": hot, cold, חם and קר are a mode only right after on, to or על in a sentence that names the AC, "מזגן על קר") does nothing. A time is never a level: a number right after at, in, for, until or עד is a time unless a unit follows ("at 7" is not understood, "at 50%" and "עד 40%" are levels), and a number with ב or מ in front ("ב-7", "בשבע", "ב-20%", "מ-7") is a time or a change by that much, never understood (ב is "at" and "by" alike: "ב-50%" may be either); a level is said alone ("kitchen lights 30") or with to, ל, על. Nothing is done until the sentence is understood whole. A scene whose name is the sentence ("All off") is that scene (a name with a time or "don't" in it only said whole, 1.10.0: ADR-066).
- **Through the existing calls only.** Each action is what a tap does, with its optimistic state, confirmation and undo: a device's `PATCH` (controls.js), `POST /v1/off` (turn-off.js, Home's Turn off all, now also exported as `turnOffNow`), `POST /v1/scenes/{id}/run`, the Sonos commands, a door's `POST /v1/relays/{id}/pulse` or a doorbell's `/open`. The controller decides as for any tap (`src/auth/access.lua`): the parser only offers what the controller listed, and a refusal shows. Nothing changes in the API, the driver or the cloud.
- **Second taps stay.** A door or gate: the command is the first tap, and the door's own Open button shows "Tap again to open" for 5 seconds, as everywhere; saying it again is never the second tap. A scene that opens doors: its Run button, which asks for its second tap. "Turn off everything" and the other whole-home commands: a confirm with what is on ("3 lights on, 1 AC on"), for 10 seconds, then Home's Turn off all.
- **Shown before and as it acts**: "Kitchen: lights off" and Working…, then Done (for 8 seconds), "Nothing to change.", "Sent, waiting for the devices to confirm." or "2 of 5 didn't work: …". The field empties once understood; words not understood stay, to correct (until the app's language changes, 1.10.0: then the field, the answer and its examples go, since they were in the other language).
- **The microphone.** Where the browser has the Web Speech API, a microphone button listens once in the app's language (`en-US`, `he-IL`), shows what it hears, and sends the final words. Of the service's three guesses the likeliest decides, a question, a problem or a refusal (don't, a time, how warm one feels) included; the others count only when it is not understood at all, the likeliest of them that says anything first, so a less likely guess never settles a question nor drops a "don't". Closing the dialog or leaving Home while it listens aborts it: nothing it heard is done, and the field gets back what was typed before; Stop ends it and does what it heard. Its errors are said in plain words (blocked, nothing heard, no microphone, no service); a browser that refuses its service (`service-not-allowed`: Siri and Dictation off) has no microphone for the rest of the visit. On iPhone and iPad in the Home Screen app there is none, and the field says "Type or dictate a command": the keyboard's microphone types into it. `app/_headers` adds `Permissions-Policy: microphone=(self), camera=(), geolocation=()` (the microphone was allowed by default; now it is said, and the app uses no camera or location of the device); the browser still asks the user first.
- **Privacy.** DirectorLink sends the words nowhere: they are understood on the device, and only the API requests leave it, sealed as before. With the microphone, the browser's own speech service hears the sound and returns the text: Chrome's sends it to Google's servers, Edge's to Microsoft's, Safari's to Apple's (Siri's recognition, on the device for some languages); the app says so in one line while it listens. The keyboard's dictation is the phone's own (on iPhone, Apple's). Nothing is stored. The controller sees the same requests as taps, the cloud nothing new (docs/ACCOUNTS.md).

**Why:** On the device and without AI, because the user's names and requests then go nowhere new, it works the moment the home answers, and a few kilobytes of rules cost a phone nothing. The user's own names rather than a fixed vocabulary of devices, because that is how a family speaks of its home, and because the names are already in the app, filtered by the controller to what this user may see. Asking rather than guessing, because a wrong light at night or a gate opened by mistake costs more than one more tap; whole sentences or nothing, because a command understood in part is a guess. The existing calls, because the controller already decides who may do what, and a second way to control the home would be a second thing to secure. The second taps kept, because a sentence is easier to say by mistake than a button is to tap twice.

**Consequence:** Sentences outside these patterns are not understood (several actions at once, relative changes such as "warmer" or "dim a bit", fan speeds, Sonos favorites and groups, refrigerators, cameras, the alarm, schedules); "I didn't understand" says so with examples. Some that a person would take as a level are not understood, to never take a time for one: "set the AC at 23", "ב-50%", "האור בחצי". A typo in a short name asks one more tap. Speech needs the browser's service and its network; the Home Screen app on iPhone uses the keyboard's dictation. A light whose name has a heating word is left out of "the lights in the room" on and to a level, which a light that only sounds like one ("Heater lamp") would be too; naming it works. No API, driver or cloud change: a 1.9.0 app with a 1.8.0 driver has the field (with what that driver lists), a 1.8.0 app none, and going back to 1.8.0 loses nothing (nothing is stored). The opt-in assistant of the roadmap, which would understand any sentence with an AI, is separate and later.

**Amended by ADR-066 (1.10.0):** two or three things in one sentence, all or nothing; changes by a step only with their own words (*brighter*, *warmer*, *louder*, *יותר אור*, *תעלה את המזגן*); and lights named for heating left as they are by every "all the lights" (on, off, a level, a step, a room's All off and Turn off all, the buttons too), one rule in `app/js/heaters.js`.

**Amended by ADR-068 (1.10.0):** Spanish and Italian too, as fully as English and Hebrew: each with its own words, read when the app is in that language and first, then English and Hebrew (understood in every language, as before) only when it understood nothing; never one language's words for another's; the rooms' names in every language of Settings → Rooms; the microphone in es-ES and it-IT; heaters by their Spanish and Italian names.

## ADR-062 — A device whose browser lost its alerts is not counted as asked; a user sees and removes the ask links of all their devices

**Context:** Two gaps the 1.8.0 reviews found beside ask before opening (ADR-058). (1) The controller counts a device as one it can ask by its alert choice `on`, which only the app sets (ADR-050). When a browser lost its push subscription (iOS drops a Home Screen app's, the browser replaces it and fires `pushsubscriptionchange`, or the user turns notifications off in the browser's settings), the 1.8.0 app forgot it without telling the controller (often it found out at start, before it had read the controller's system), and the account service deleted the subscriptions its push service no longer knew (404, 410) without telling anyone. An ask link's run then answered `asked` ("answer the notification on your phone") and History said "Asked on 1 device" while no device could get the question. (2) A user who made a link on a phone that was then lost could not remove it from their new device: the door's Ask screen showed only this device's link, and the list of links is for admins, although the API already let a key see and remove its user's links.

**Decision (1.9.0):**
- **The app tells the controller.** When it finds this device's alerts gone (no subscription, and none can be made without a tap, at start or when the service worker hears `pushsubscriptionchange`; the permission taken back; the account service refusing the registration), it shows alerts off, removes the browser from the account service and tells the controller, `PUT /v1/alerts/choices {"on": false}`. When the controller cannot be told yet (its system not read, the home out of reach), the app keeps a note (`directorlink.alerts.lost`: the home and the key id) and tells it once it can, a minute between tries; switching alerts on again, another home or another key, or forgetting the key ends the note. The service worker has no key: it only tells the open app (`directorlink-push-changed`), and a closed app does it at its next start. What the app tells the controller goes one request after another, and a start that finds alerts gone never says they are on again.
- **The account service tells the controller.** A new relay message, `{"type":"alerts_gone","id","keys":[…]}` (docs/RELAY.md), only to a driver whose hello lists `alerts_gone`: after each `keys` message and whenever browsers are removed (an app turning alerts off, an account leaving or signing out everywhere, a registration that replaced a key's browser or pruned an account's oldest), the controller's keys with no browser registered by an account that uses them; after a `notify`, the keys it named that had none, or whose every browser the push service no longer knew; after the cloud's own pushes (offline, schedule, a device asking to join), the keys whose last browser went so. Key ids only, at most 200. The driver (`Alerts.gone`, `src/cloud/alerts.lua`) switches those keys' `on` off, keeps their kinds, saves it and logs it; it ignores ids it does not have and answers nothing. An app that still has alerts on says so again at its next start (ADR-050).
- **A user's links on all their devices.** `GET /v1/ask-links` (and the link `POST` makes) also says `device`, the name of the key that made it, and `this_user`, whether it was made on a device of the asking key's user. The door's Ask screen lists, below this device's link, the links of the user's other devices for that door, each with its device, label and last run, and **Remove** (asked first: "Remove the link on Avi's old phone for Main gate?"), for members and admins alike. With a 1.8.0 controller, which says neither, all the links a member gets are theirs, and an admin has the list. The list on Scenes → Links for automations stays the admins', and now says each link's device.

**Why:** The controller's `on` is what makes it seal to a device and count it, so it must follow the browser. The app knows first; the account service knows what the app could not say (an app never opened again, a subscription that died at the push service). Both, because neither sees everything. Key ids only, because the cloud already knows which keys have subscriptions (`member_keys`, ADR-050); what it says can only lower what a device gets, an alert the cloud could withhold anyway, never anyone's access. At `keys` too, so that browsers that went while the home was away count from its next connection. The door's screen, because that is where a user makes and manages their links, and every link of theirs is for a door they can open there.

**Consequence:** What the cloud learns: nothing new. The controller learns that the account service has no browser for some of its keys. A run right after a subscription died at the push service still answers `asked`, as nobody knew yet; the push that finds it gone tells the controller, and the runs after answer `nobody`. A device the cloud reported gone that still has alerts (a new registration at that very moment) gets nothing sealed until its app's next start says they are on. An app before 1.9.0 tells nothing, but the account service does; a 1.8.0 Worker never sends `alerts_gone`, and a 1.8.0 driver is never sent it (its hello does not list it; it would ignore it). Going back to 1.8.0 loses nothing: the choices store is the same (only `on` changed), and a 1.8.0 app ignores the new fields of `/v1/ask-links`. No D1 migration; the Worker and the driver can be deployed in either order.

## ADR-061 — Users and their devices: one set of permissions a user, up to five devices, the devices of one account one user when an admin confirms it, pairing at home for a chosen user

**Context:** 1.8.0 (ADR-054) set roles and permissions per person (a profile), but a person was still made per device: pairing, an admin's key and every invitation for someone else made a new one, named after the device. On the owner's controller, four keys are four people: the owner's PC ("Chrome on Windows", the owner by the oldest-admin rule), the owner's iPhone (an admin of its own, in a person named "Chrome on iPhone"), and two Android members. The word "people" said nothing about it, and nothing tied a person to who they are: two devices signed in to the same Google account could have different permissions, and nothing limited how many devices one person held. Remote access already needs an account: the cloud passes a device's sealed requests only for an account that belongs to the home, and records which account uses which key (`member_keys`), but the controller never learns it. Only admins could add a device (Add my other device, Join from another device, ADR-053, approved only on an admin's device), and only Composer made pairing codes, each a new admin.

**Decision (1.9.0):**
- **Users.** "People" are **users** in every word people see (the app in English and Hebrew, the docs, History). The API keeps its names (`/v1/profiles`, `profile_id`, `/v1/api-keys`); new routes say user. One user is one set of permissions (ADR-054's admin and member, unchanged), for all their devices; two people who share one Google account are one user.
- **An account for remote access.** A user reaches the home away from it through a Google or Apple account on their devices, as before: the cloud passes requests only for an account that belongs to the home, which it joins by an invitation (or the claim). A user without an account is allowed (a child's iPad, a kitchen tablet, a script): the home network only. An admin invites the account of a user paired at home into that user (`POST /v1/invitations` with `profile_id`: the device that opens the link joins them, with their permissions). One account belongs to one user at a home: an admin brings the devices of one account into one user (below).
- **Up to five devices a user** (`Users.DEVICE_LIMIT`, `src/auth/users.lua`): keys that are not revoked or expired (both are gone from the keys' store). Every way a key gets into a user refuses a sixth with `409 USER_DEVICE_LIMIT`: pairing with a code made for that user (said at the start of a CPace exchange, before anything counts against the code, and again before the code is used), an invitation for them (`for_me` or `profile_id`) when it is made and when it is used (the join answers `USER_DEVICE_LIMIT`, and the invitation stays, so that it works once a device is removed), `POST /v1/api-keys` with `profile_id`, moving a key (`PATCH /v1/api-keys/{id}` `profile_id`) and bringing an account's devices together. The problem carries `limit`, `user` (`id`, `name`; not to a caller without a key, a device pairing before its code is checked, which learns nothing of the user a code is for) and, for a caller who sees that user (an admin, or the user themself), `devices`: each with `last_used_at`, whether it is this one and whether the caller may remove it; the app says "Remove a device first" with that list and Remove where allowed (`views/device-limit.js`), also when approving a device that asks to join. A restore brings the backup's users back as they were and the restoring device takes the place of one of the backup's keys (`replaces_key`) or stays a user of its own, so it never gives a user a sixth. A user who had more before 1.9.0 keeps them and gets none until they have fewer than five. When each key was last used is kept across restarts now (`directorlink_keys_last_used`, written at most once an hour a key), so that the list says which device is unused.
- **Settings → Users** (`#/access`, `GET /v1/users`, role `member`): each user with their name, Admin or Member and their access (1.8.0's editor), whether their devices use an account (the owner sees the accounts' emails, which the cloud lists for the owner only; a device sees its own; anyone else "Google or Apple account" or "No Google or Apple account: home network only"), and under them every device with when it was last used and Remove. Admins see every user, the suggestions to bring an account's devices together, the invitations and (the owner) the accounts and requests to join; a member sees only their own user. An app with a controller before 1.9.0 shows 1.8.0's People and devices, for admins.
- **The devices of one account become one user when an admin confirms it** (`src/auth/accounts.lua`, `src/auth/users.lua`). The controller learns which keys share an account from the cloud: a driver whose `hello` lists `users` is sent `{"type":"accounts","id","keys":{"<key id>":["<tag>", …]}}` after each `keys` message it sent, after an account's first sealed request with a key, and after a join, a member removed, a new owner, or an account deleted or left without a sign-in (`docs/RELAY.md`). A tag is the first 16 hex digits of SHA-256(`DirectorLink account v1|<home id>|<account id>`): per account and home, never the account's id or email, and another home gets another tag (account ids are 128 random bits, so a tag cannot be worked back). Only keys with exactly one account count: a device used by several accounts (a shared tablet) stays where an admin put it. When an account's devices are in two users or more, that is a suggestion in Settings → Users ("DirectorLink's servers say these devices use the same account: make them one user?"), never a merge by itself, not even between users with the same permissions: **every merge asks** (the owner's decision of 2026-10-05), so that DirectorLink's servers can never change anything alone. An admin confirms it, choosing whose permissions stay (`POST /v1/users/merge` `{account, keep, revision}`). The suggestion lists each user with Admin or Member (or Owner) and their devices of that account, and offers the user whose access is within every other's (`Access.within`: never an admin over a member; none when neither has less, and the admin chooses); the confirmation says whose access, language, theme and favorites the moved devices then have, and what an admin's device becomes. The confirmation names the suggestion's `revision`, a digest of what was shown (its devices in each user, and each user's role, ownership and permissions): when the account service changed the group since, or anyone changed those users, nothing moves (`409 SUGGESTION_CHANGED`) and the app shows it again; every rule is asked again at the confirmation. With the owner's user only the owner confirms (`403 OWNER_PROTECTED`; another admin's device names the owner's user and devices to confirm it on), and the owner's user is the one that stays (`400` on `keep`): the owner's devices never move. There is always an admin (`409 LAST_ADMIN`), and five devices at most (`409 USER_DEVICE_LIMIT`). The devices that move get the kept user's permissions and 1.7.0 role, in one write; a device that is no longer an admin's loses the invitations it made (as when its user is made a member); a user left without devices goes, and the favorites they had are added to the kept user's. Every merge is logged and in History (`access` `users_merged`, with the admin who confirmed it), and every new suggestion once (`merge_suggested`, remembered by its devices whatever tag the account is given; again only if it went and came back; at most ten a day). The tags are written at most once a minute, and only when they changed. No "keep apart": a suggestion stays until it is confirmed or no longer applies (a device removed or moved, an account gone). An admin may still move a device to another user; if its account's other devices stay elsewhere, the suggestion comes back.
- **A device of user U signs in with an account that belongs to user V:** it is then one of that account's devices (its only account) in another user than the account's others: a suggestion, and an admin chooses V's or U's permissions for that account's devices, even when U and V have the same. Only that account's devices move: U keeps its other devices (one without an account, or with another account). A device that already had another account (U's own) has two now: a shared device, which stays in U.
- **Empty users.** A user goes with their last device, as a profile did (its name, permissions and preferences; logged), and so do the invitations and the pairing code made for them (an invitation into a user who is gone would make a new user of whoever opened it: it is refused as `INVITATION_NOT_FOUND`).
- **Pairing at home asks which user the device is for, on the admin's side.** An admin makes a pairing code in the app (`POST /v1/pairing-code`, `{profile_id}` or `{name, role, access}`, Settings → Users: Pair a device on a user, or New user at home): the code is Composer's kind (8 digits, 15 minutes, works once, the same limits, CPace; one code at a time, shown in Composer too, "made in the app for one user"), and the device that pairs with it joins that user with their permissions, or becomes the new user with the name and access chosen; the device never chooses (the pairing request takes no user). It works only while its user is there and its maker is still an admin who may add a device to them (the owner's user only the owner, `Access.mayChangePerson`): otherwise `403 PAIRING_NOT_ACTIVE` and the code closes. `DELETE /v1/pairing-code` closes it. Composer's New Pairing Code still makes a new admin user (the installer's way in), and replaces a code made in the app.
- **Members add and remove their own devices.** `DELETE /v1/api-keys/{keyId}` is a member's route: a member removes the other devices of their own user (another user's device is, for them, `404` like one that does not exist); an admin any device but the owner's, which only the owner removes (`Access.mayRemoveDevice`). `POST /v1/invitations` is a member's route: every user invites their own other device (`for_me`: Add my other device in Settings → Account), into their own user with their permissions, within five; inviting anyone else stays the admins' (`403 FORBIDDEN`). A member revokes the invitations their own user's devices made (`DELETE /v1/invitations/{id}`). A member's own invitation lasts 10 minutes at most, and their user keeps two waiting at most (a new one replaces the oldest), so that a member never fills the controller's 20. With an email it is bound only to an account that already uses the member's device at the home: the controller asks the account service to register it for that key's account (`for_key`, docs/RELAY.md), which checks that the email is one of an account that uses that key there (`member_keys`) and answers with the key; otherwise `403 ACCOUNT_NOT_OF_DEVICE`, and `502 FOR_KEY_UNSUPPORTED` from an account service that does not say it checked. So a member never brings another account into the home: an admin invites it. The join of a member's `for_me` invitation is checked again when it is used, as ADR-054 checks an admin's (the maker still a key of that user, `Access.mayChangePerson`), and an invitation for a new user is joined only while its maker is still an admin. **Join from another device** is approved on any device of the same account at that home, whatever its role: the app offers Show code to a member's device when the controller says `features.users`; the cloud lets an account ask when one of its keys is known at the home, any key when the home's last `hello` listed `users` (kept by the home's Durable Object as `driver_features`), admin keys as before otherwise; and the join push of ADR-059 goes to that account's browsers at the home registered with any of its keys, not only admins'. Settings → Controller → Alerts on this device shows its switch ("A new device of mine asks to join") to members too. A member never moves a device into another user (`PATCH /v1/api-keys` stays admin) nor gives a device more than their own permissions.
- **API.** `GET /v1/users`, `POST /v1/users/merge` (with the suggestion's `revision`; `409 SUGGESTION_CHANGED`), `POST` and `DELETE /v1/pairing-code`; `profile_id` in `POST /v1/invitations` and in invitations; `features.users` in `GET /v1/system`; `USER_DEVICE_LIMIT` with `limit`, `user`, `devices`; `ACCOUNT_NOT_OF_DEVICE`; History `access` `pairing_code`, `users_merged`, `merge_suggested`.
- **Backups.** The people section (ADR-054) is unchanged: a user's permissions go by profile, as before. Account tags are not secrets, but they are not in backups: the account service sends them again at the next connection, and a restore keeps none for keys that did not come back. When keys were last used is not in backups either.

**Why:** One user per person, because an admin decides about people, and a person's phone and computer must never differ; the device was the unit only because pairing and invitations made one. The account as what ties devices to a user, because it is what the person proves when they sign in, and the cloud already knows which account uses which key: an opaque tag per home gives the controller exactly that, and nothing that names the person or links two homes. Every merge asked, because a tag is the cloud's word: if the cloud could make devices join a user with more permissions, it could give itself (or a stolen database) an admin's device; and even between users with the same permissions a merge gives each the other's devices to see and remove, their ask links and the questions those links ask (the 1.9.0 cloud review), so nothing is merged until an admin confirms it, the owner for the owner, and the confirmation is for exactly what the admin saw (the revision), as ADR-029 requires: the cloud never changes anyone's access, or anyone's devices, on its own. The user with less access offered first, because a kid signed in on a parent's iPad must not become an admin with one tap. A member's own invitation bound to an account of the member's device, because inviting anyone else is the admins': otherwise a member, or whoever copies a member's key, could bring any account into the home. Moving only the devices of that account, not whole users, because a user may also hold a device without an account (a child's iPad an admin put there), which the account says nothing about. Five devices, because a family member has a phone, a tablet, a computer and a Home Screen app or two; more is a key nobody uses, and the limit makes the list of devices worth reading. A pairing code for a chosen user on the admin's side, because the device that pairs is the one that must not choose: it would choose an admin. Members adding their own devices within their own user, because it gives nobody more than they have, and the controller still checks every step.

**Consequence:** What the controller learns from the cloud: for each of its keys that an account uses, an opaque tag per account, so which of its keys share an account, and how many accounts use a key; never who, nor an email, nor anything about another home. What the cloud learns from the controller: nothing new (the same `keys` message, the same sealed requests); it already knew which account uses which key, and its tags are its own computation. It sends one more message to each driver after every change of keys. Someone holding the cloud's database could send false tags: at most a suggestion an admin must confirm, which the app shows as the servers' word, with each user's role, and which is refused if it changed after the admin looked; and History entries, ten a day at most. The owner's devices never move. What the cloud learns of a member's own invitation: that it is for the member's own key (it knew the key ids, and which account uses which). A 1.8.0 driver after a downgrade: it reads the keys, profiles and people stores as they are (a merged user is one profile with all its keys; a user with up to five devices), ignores `directorlink_accounts` and `directorlink_keys_last_used`, makes new people per device again, has no limit and no pairing codes from the app (a code is in memory only), lets no member approve a join or make an invitation (a member's `for_me` invitation made in 1.9.0 is refused at its join, as its maker is no admin), and joins an invitation with `profile_id` into that profile as it does an admin's `for_me` (and lists it as the admin's own other device; its `save()` drops `for_user`, so back on 1.9.0 such an invitation shows as the admin's `for_me`, and still joins that user). A 1.8.0 app against 1.9.0: People and devices works as before (the routes and fields are all there), members see no Users and approve nothing, and a refused sixth device shows the controller's sentence. A 1.9.0 app against 1.8.0: People and devices, for admins (`features.users` is missing). A 1.8.0 Worker with a 1.9.0 driver sends no tags (Users says nothing about accounts, and suggests nothing), lets only admin keys' accounts ask to join, and cannot check a member's own invitation with an email (refused, `FOR_KEY_UNSUPPORTED`); a 1.9.0 Worker with a 1.8.0 driver sends it nothing new. On the owner's controller after the update: once the relay sends the tags, the PC's and the iPhone's users share the owner's account and are a suggestion only the owner confirms, on the PC (the iPhone says so; the owner's user stays, so the iPhone gets the owner's permissions, language, theme and favorites, and the iPhone's user goes); the Android members are left alone, and members who share an account are a suggestion too. Members open the 1.9 app once so that it shows them join requests (a cached 1.8.0 app gets the push and shows nothing). No D1 migration. Not yet seen on a real controller: the tags arriving after the first connection, and the suggestion on the owner's home.

## ADR-060 — A demo home on the website

**Context:** People who don't have DirectorLink yet can only look at screenshots before asking their installer for it. The owner wants a page where anyone can try the app and see how easy it is. The website runs only its own files (CSP `script-src 'self'`, `connect-src` only for DirectorLink in numbers, ADR-052), and its showcase is English, with a made-up home and no Jewish calendar features.

**Decision (website, after 1.8.0):** `directorlink.io/try/` shows a phone with the app's screens (its graphite palette, icons and layout) and a made-up home: the rooms, family (Alex, Jordan, Sam, Robin) and drawn camera pictures of the 1.8.0 screenshots. Beside it, six things to try (turn on a light, cool the living room, run Good Night, open the main gate with two taps, play music in two rooms, look at a camera), each ticked off when done, with **Show me** to open the right screen and mark what to tap, and the links to download the driver and open the app. The demo is its own small script (`site/try/demo.js`), not the app: everything happens in the visitor's browser; it talks to nothing (no address in it but the SVG namespace, no `fetch`), stores nothing, sets no markup but its own icons, and is in English. `scripts/check_sites.py` holds those rules, lets only `try/index.html` load it, and checks every camera's two pictures are there. Every page's header links to it (**Try it**), and the home page's first button is **Try the demo**.

**Why:** Trying beats a screenshot, and a demo that cannot reach anything cannot leak or break anything. Its own script rather than the app in a demo mode, because the app's code would then carry a fake controller and a mode that must never be reached on a real home.

**Consequence:** The demo copies the app's look and must be kept close to it by hand when screens change (the icons are copied from `app/js/icons.js`). With 1.9.0 its Settings shows Users (ADR-061): each with their account, or none and the home network only, and their devices, up to five, with Remove. No new address, cookie or storage on the website; the CSP is unchanged.

## ADR-059 — Older drivers can be turned away; a device whose driver is updated is set up again; favorites of removed devices

**Context:** Three gaps the owner met in 1.7.0, and one that has not happened yet. (1) If a flaw is found in the remote protocol, a fixed driver protects nobody while old drivers keep connecting: the account service learns each home's DirectorLink version (`X-DirectorLink-Version` on `/relay/connect`, the hello's `version`) but does nothing with it. (2) The owner updated the Samsung Refrigerator driver to 1.1.0 in Composer; it added REPORTED_VARIABLES and TEMPERATURE_UNIT (ADR-049), which the refrigerator adapter reads only when it sets the device up, so nothing changed until a Refresh Project. Snap One's list of system events has OnDriverAdded and OnDriverDisabled, nothing for an update, and none of the project events DirectorLink watches came. Director documents `C4:GetDeviceData(id, tag)` from OS 2.10, which reads a driver's `<devicedata>`, its `<version>` among them: the number Composer compares to update a driver. (3) The owner replaced cameras in Composer; the app kept asking for two removed camera ids (404) and showed empty tiles, because a person's favorites (`"kind:id"` in their profile) outlive their devices. A discovery that fails, or a device missing for a moment while its driver is replaced, must not cost anyone their favorites.

**Decision (1.8.0):**
- **A minimum driver version.** The Worker var `MIN_DRIVER_VERSION` (`"1.8.0"`; unset or empty: none; set in `cloud/wrangler.jsonc` → `vars` and deployed, never only in Cloudflare's dashboard, whose vars the next `wrangler deploy` replaces with the file's) is checked by the Worker on `/relay/connect`, before the home's Durable Object is asked (`cloud/src/min-version.js`): a driver below it is answered `426` Problem Details, `code` `DRIVER_UPDATE_REQUIRED`, with `minimum_version`. Versions compare by their three numbers (`1.8.0-rc.1` is 1.8.0, `1.10.0` is above 1.8.0); a version without three numbers (`dev`, none) is below any minimum; a minimum that is not three numbers is ignored and logged (`min_driver_version_invalid`), so a typo cannot cut every home off. Each refusal is logged (`driver_refused`, the home and the version). The home's object still has the version of the driver's last connection: while no driver is connected and that version is below the minimum, a sealed request for the home is answered `503 HOME_UPDATE_REQUIRED` instead of `HOME_OFFLINE`, the home's status says `update_required` and `minimum_version`, and `GET /v1/homes` marks the home `update_required`; the app then says "Update DirectorLink: the version on your controller can no longer connect to remote access". A scene link's run still gets `503 HOME_OFFLINE` (a phone's automation shows no reason).
- **Drivers from 1.8.0 understand the refusal** (`src/cloud/relay.lua`): Remote Status says "Update DirectorLink: this version can no longer connect to remote access", the next attempt is an hour later (`UPDATE_RETRY_SECONDS`, in case the minimum is lowered again), the log says it at each refusal (warn, with the minimum), and the history has one entry, `system` `remote_update_required` (`from` this version, `to` the minimum when given), until a connection opens again; switching Remote Access off and on adds none. *Refinement:* until then `GET /v1/remote` says `update_required: true` and `minimum_version`, so that the app on the home network, which never asks the account service for the home, says "Update DirectorLink…" in Settings → Account → This home (in place of Link this home, before the home is linked). **Drivers before 1.8.0** take the 426 as any other refusal: they keep trying with their backoff (every minute), their Remote Status says `Reconnecting in 60 s (refused: DRIVER_UPDATE_REQUIRED)`, and nothing goes into their history. The home network is never affected: the minimum is the account service's only.
- **A device whose driver is updated is set up again** (`src/control4/driver_updates.lua`, `src/adapters/manager.lua` `setUpAgain`). When a project read sets the devices up, DirectorLink reads the `<version>` of the driver behind every device an adapter took (its protocol drivers, else the device itself; one Director call a driver), and from the scheduler's minute tick it reads 25 of them again, one after another (a home with 200 drivers: each within 8 minutes). A driver whose version changed has every device an adapter took on it set up again, exactly as a project refresh does for each (its variables, listeners, capabilities, and what a refresh keeps: a relay's last state, a doorbell's rings); the variables it was watched on are let go first, so none is delivered twice; nothing else is touched, and the project is not read again. Each device is logged (`a device's driver was updated in Composer; set up again`, with the driver, the versions and whether it works now). A device whose adapter could not set it up before (a refrigerator driver missing a variable) is tried again too. *Refinement:* Director may show the new version while it is still starting the updated driver, before that driver has added its variables; a device that could not be set up then is tried again 1, 2 and 5 minutes later (`RETRY_MINUTES`), each try logged, and after the last one is left to Refresh Project (logged, warn). A Director that gives no versions is logged once ("a driver updated in Composer is read again only by Refresh Project").
- **Favorites of removed devices** (`src/core/favorites_gone.lua`, docs/PREFERENCES.md). Only a project read that worked counts; a read that failed, or one in which Director lists no devices at all (as while it loads a project), changes nothing. The first such read in which no device has a favorite's id marks it gone, with the time (and the name and room it had, when the read before knew the device); a later read with the device clears the mark. `GET` and `PATCH /v1/profile` give the caller's own `gone_favorites` (`entry`, `since`, and `name` when the person may see such a device there, as `Access.canSee` says). The app shows each as "Removed in Composer" with **Remove**, in place of a tile that asked for a device that is not there, and never calls a favorite removed on its own lists' say (they leave out what a person may not see, or are a minute old). Seven days after the mark, a read or the scheduler's minute tick removes the favorite from every profile (a new `version` for each), logged; only once a read in the current run of the driver has looked at the marks, since one kept from before a restart may be of a device that came back meanwhile. A kind DirectorLink does not know (another version's) is never marked or removed. The marks are kept in `directorlink_favorites_gone`.

**Why:** The minimum in the Worker, because an old driver retrying every minute must cost one Worker request, not a Durable Object wake, and the refusal must come before the old driver can use the flaw; 426 and a code, because a driver needs to tell "update" from "wrong secret". The object's last version for the app, because the Worker never lets the old driver reach it, so the object's record is that home's last word; and a change of minimum needs a deploy, which ends every connection, so each driver is checked again. An hour between attempts for 1.8.0 drivers, because an update takes a person at Composer, and the minimum may also be lowered. The version check for driver updates, because Director announces none, and `<version>` is what Composer itself compares; a few drivers a minute, because one Director call each is cheap, and all at once every few minutes would be a burst of some 200. Set up again device by device rather than a project refresh, because a refresh reads the whole project (over a thousand Director calls) for a change of one driver. Seven days for favorites, because a device replaced in Composer is gone for good, while one removed by mistake, or missing while its driver is replaced, comes back within days; the controller says which are gone because only it knows the project, and the app must not wipe what a person sees only partly.

**Consequence:** What the account service learns: nothing new. It knew each home's driver version (the header and the hello) before; it now refuses some, and says so to the home's own accounts. A driver whose version cannot be read as three numbers is refused once a minimum is set (a build from the repository says `dev`; packaged ones carry the release's number). With a minimum set, a 1.7.0 driver retries every minute until updated, each a refused Worker request (about 1,440 a day a home); a 1.7.0 app shows the 503's code in its general words ("The request through your account did not work (HOME_UPDATE_REQUIRED)"). Driver updates: an update that keeps the driver's version number is not seen (Refresh Project does it), and one is seen up to a few minutes late; a driver Director gives no version is not watched. Favorites: a device removed in Composer stays a "Removed in Composer" tile for up to seven days unless someone removes it; one removed and added back gets a new id from Control4, so it is a new device and its old favorite goes; DirectorLink 1.7.0 ignores the marks and shows nothing of them, and a favorite dropped by 1.8.0 is gone in 1.7.0 too (it named a device that is not there). No D1 migration; the Worker and the driver can be deployed in either order (a 1.7.0 Worker never refuses, and a 1.8.0 Worker without the var refuses nothing).

## ADR-058 — Ask before opening: a door's link that asks its person, and only their tap opens; Siri and Google Assistant with scene links

**Context:** Scene links never open doors or gates (ADR-051): a link is a bearer secret that works from anywhere, leaks easily (a shared screenshot, a synced Shortcut), and a phone's geofence fires early, late or twice. The owner still wants to arrive at the gate hands-free, safely. The roadmap also promised running scenes by voice: Siri and Google Assistant can run what the scene link screen already sets up, if the app says how.

**Decision (1.8.0):**
- **A link that asks.** A door or gate (a relay DirectorLink knows) gets links of a new kind, kept apart from scene links (`src/core/ask_links.lua`, store `directorlink_ask_links`, which 1.7.0 never reads): one per door and key, made by a key that may open that door (`Access.canOpen`), with Door Control on, Remote Access on and the home linked (`403 FORBIDDEN`, `403 DOOR_CONTROL_DISABLED`, `409 REMOTE_ACCESS_OFF`, `409 HOME_NOT_LINKED`); at most 50 (`409 ASK_LINK_LIMIT_REACHED`). `GET`, `POST /v1/ask-links` (`{relay_id, label}`) and `DELETE /v1/ask-links/{linkId}`, role `member` (ADR-054: making a link asks `Access.canOpen` for that door); a key sees and removes its person's links, an admin everyone's (another person's is 404 for others). The secret is a scene link's: 160 bits, kept as a hash, shown once (ADR-051). In the app: **Ask** on the door's row in its room opens its screen (`#/door/<id>/ask`), with the secret once, Copy buttons and the steps (iPhone Arrive automation, Siri, Android, Google Assistant, a browser); Scenes → Links for automations lists them below the scene links, with Remove for other people's (since 1.9.0 the door's screen also lists the links of the user's other devices, with Remove, ADR-062). *Refinement:* one link per door and **key**, not per person: each device's automation holds its own secret, so making one on the iPad does not break the iPhone's; the person asked is the key's (its profile), and the link goes with that key.
- **Its run asks; it opens nothing.** The same address and run as a scene link's (`/run/<home>.<link>`, POST, the secret in the body, the relay's `link` message, the Worker's 30 runs a minute a home and its wrong-guess limit, the controller's 6 a minute a link), plus at most 10 runs an hour a link that ask or say why nobody was asked (`asked`, `nobody`, `doors_off`, `not_asked`; a `waiting` run and a refused one do not count). *Refinement (review):* the phone is told the real wait: the controller's `retry_s` (up to an hour) becomes `Retry-After`, and the words say it ("This link asked too often in the last hour; it can ask again in 50 minutes"; the browser's page the same), where the minute's limit says "try again in a minute". The controller checks the link again (the key that made it, `Access.canOpen(that key, the door)`, the door, the home), then answers `link_result` with a result word: `doors_off` (Door Control off), `waiting` (a request for this door and person is still open: nothing new is sent), `nobody` (no device of the person that may open the door has alerts on; since 1.9.0 the app and the account service say when a browser's are gone, ADR-062), `not_asked` (the alert could not be sent now) or `asked`. Asking makes a request (an id of 16 hex digits, in memory only, for 120 s) and sends one `notify` sealed as ADR-050's alerts, kind `open_request`, `brief`, with the door's id, name and room, the request's id, its seconds and the link's label, to the keys of that person that may open the door and switched alerts on. It is not one of the kinds a key chooses: the person made the link to be asked. The phone gets `200 {"result", "message"}` ("Asked: answer the notification on your phone to open."). *Refinement:* at most one open request per door **and person**, not per door: otherwise one person's leaked link, run every two minutes, would keep everyone else's question from being sent.
- **The answer opens, with the device's own key.** The service worker shows "Open Main gate?" ("Your link “Arriving home” asked at 07:15. Tap to answer.", tag `open-<door>`); its tap opens `#/open/<door>/<request>/<until>`, `until` being when it came on the device's clock plus the request's seconds. The screen asks "Open Main gate?" with **Open** and **Cancel**. Open is the door's pulse, `POST /v1/relays/{id}/pulse` with `{"request": "<id>"}`, sealed with that device's key as every request: checked as any opening (the route's role, `Access.canOpen`, Door Control, pulse only, never a hold), and only from a key the request was sent to, before it expires, once (`409 OPEN_REQUEST_EXPIRED` for an unknown, expired, other door's or other device's request, `409 OPEN_REQUEST_ANSWERED`; nothing opens). Cancel sends nothing: the request ends by itself. Past `until` the screen shows no Open, only the door's room. *Refinement:* the pulse route with the request's id rather than a route of its own, so that every rule for opening a door (the roles builder's included) applies unchanged; a body without `request` is ignored, as before.
- **History.** `door` `asked`, by the link (`who.type` `link`, its label), with how many devices were asked, or with `outcome` skipped and `reason` `nobody`, `doors_off` or `not_sent`; then the opening that answers it, `door` `pulse` by the answering key ("opened by Dana · Dana's iPad"), with `ids.link_id` and the label in `note`. `access` `ask_link_created`, `ask_link_replaced`, `ask_link_removed` (reasons `key_gone`, `no_access`, `door_gone`, `other_home`). A refused run and `waiting` are no entries.
- **Revocation.** As scene links: the key that made it revoked or expired, Composer's Revoke All API Keys, Remove All Scene Links and Reset Remote Identity (counted in their `links_removed`). Also when its person may no longer open the door (a role change, and in 1.8.0 a person's doors or rooms: `Access.canOpen` is asked whenever keys change and at every run) and when the door is gone from the project (only once a project was read). Not in backups: a link is a minute's work to make again, and a restore keeps the controller's links as they are (pruned when their key did not come back).
- **Siri and Google Assistant.** A new scene link's screen also has **Siri** (a shortcut, not an automation, with the same Get Contents of URL step, named like the scene: "Hey Siri, Good night") and **Google Assistant** (the request in HTTP Shortcuts, Tasker or MacroDroid named like the scene, started by an Assistant routine), in English and Hebrew; an existing link's screen says it in one line. The ask link's screen has the same, with "Open Main gate". No new API. *Refinement (review):* an automation that runs at once shows nothing by itself, and a failed ask means standing at a closed gate: the ask link's iPhone steps end with an optional **Show Notification** with Contents of URL, and its Siri steps with an optional **Show Result** (Siri says it), in English and Hebrew.
- **Older and newer.** `features.ask_links` in `GET /v1/system`; without it the app shows no Ask. The Worker gives the new result words their messages; a 1.7.0 Worker answers them 404, so it is deployed before or with the driver. A 1.7.0 driver answers an ask link's run NOT_FOUND (it does not know the id).

**Why:** Asking keeps the convenience (the phone's automation or Siri starts it, one tap on the notification ends it) without making the link a key: the link only starts a question, and the answer is an ordinary sealed opening by a device the person holds, checked as any. Sealed to the person's own keys, so the cloud delivers a question it cannot read, about a door it cannot name. A short request, used once and only by the devices it was sent to, so a stale notification tapped later or forwarded opens nothing. The run's limits, so a leaked link costs at most ten alerts of the home's sixty an hour.

**Consequence:** What the cloud learns, beyond ADR-051's run (the home, the link's id, when, the status): the result word, so which link ids are ask links and whether their person was asked, and right after an `asked`, a brief `notify` for that person's key ids, so it can tie the link to the keys (and accounts) it asks; brief notices are now rings or questions. Never which door; whether it was opened only as far as a sealed request from that person's device soon after the question suggests it (the opening is a sealed request like any). A leaked link makes the owner's phone ask, at most ten times an hour, and fills History with those entries; replace or remove it. The question needs alerts on the device (and on iPhone the Home Screen app): without them the run answers `nobody` (in 1.8.0 only while the controller's alert choice was right: a browser that had lost its subscription still counted as asked, fixed by ADR-062). A push is kept a minute by the push services and the request lasts two: a phone offline longer gets no question. Requests are in memory only: a driver restart ends them. An app before 1.8.0 shows the question in the general words and its tap opens History, where nothing opens it. Going back to 1.7.0 keeps the links' store unread; its history shows `asked` and the link entries as "Something DirectorLink did". No D1 migration. Not yet seen on a real phone: the Arrive automation, the question's tap opening the Home Screen app on its screen, Siri running a Get Contents of URL shortcut, and an Assistant routine starting an HTTP app's request.

## ADR-057 — Sonos in scenes (a favorite at a volume, a volume, resume) and in several rooms (groups); amends ADR-044

**Context:** A scene's music step could only pause or stop (ADR-044). The owner wants "Good morning" to start a radio station in the kitchen at a quiet volume, and the music back after a pause. And one day he played music in the kitchen, then pressed play in the living room: the kitchen stopped. DirectorLink had sent Play only to the living room, which then played its own queue from the same account, and a music service plays one stream per account at a time. The Sonos app avoids this by grouping: the living room joins the kitchen's group and plays the same stream in step. ADR-044 had left grouping out, to send the players as little as possible.

**Decision (1.8.0):**
- **Groups, as the Sonos app makes them, with two actions more.** A room joins another room's group with AVTransport `SetAVTransportURI` whose address is `x-rincon:<coordinator's id>`, sent to the room that joins (as the Sonos app, SoCo and Home Assistant do); it leaves its group with AVTransport `BecomeCoordinatorOfStandaloneGroup`, sent to that room. The address is made in one place (`Protocol.groupUri`, which takes only a player's id), and the coordinator must be a player DirectorLink found in the household's zone group state: an API request names Sonos rooms by id, never an address, and an unknown room is `404`. Already in that group, or on its own already: nothing is sent. The change shows at once and the zone group state is read again at the next tick (2 s), which says what Sonos did. `scripts/check_package.py` holds the list of actions (these two added, nothing else: no `DelegateGroupCoordinationTo`, no GroupRenderingControl, no alarms or settings) and fails the build if anything but the protocol file makes an `x-rincon:` address or anything but the Sonos module uses it.
- **A group's volume is each room's own.** `PATCH /v1/music/{id}/group {"volume"}` sets each room's volume with RenderingControl `SetVolume` (already allowed), keeping their balance as the Sonos app does: each is scaled by the same factor, from the volumes they had when the group's volume was first changed (kept while their volumes are the ones last set there and the group is the same), so down to 0 and back up returns to the same balance. The group's volume shown is the average of its rooms' (`group.volume`, and `groups` in `GET /v1/music`).
- **API:** `POST /v1/music/{id}/group {"with": "<id>"}` (join the group of that room), `DELETE /v1/music/{id}/group` (leave), `PATCH /v1/music/{id}/group {"volume"}`; `GET /v1/music` adds `groups` (each coordinator, its rooms, what it plays, its volume). Members and admins; `features.sonos_groups` in `GET /v1/system` says the driver has all of this, the scene steps below too.
- **Who may.** Every Sonos room a command changes goes through `Access.canControl(actor, { kind = "music", id = <the Sonos room>, room_id = <the Control4 room it is shown in> })` (ADR-054), and a command on a group needs every room in it: play, pause, skip and a favorite on a group, its volume, leaving it, and joining (the rooms of both groups). A room's own volume and mute need that room only. Refused: `403 FORBIDDEN` naming the rooms.
- **Scene steps.** A `music` step also takes `{"action": "resume"}` (each group with a room there that is paused or stopped plays again; a radio station a scene stopped starts again; one that plays, or has nothing to play, is left alone), `{"action": "volume", "volume": 0-100}` (each Sonos room there, its own) and `{"action": "play_favorite", "favorite": {"id"}, "volume"?, "with_room_ids"?}` in a room (required). A favorite plays in the Sonos rooms of the step's room, the first leading: it leaves a group it follows (one it leads keeps its rooms, which play it too, as in the Sonos app), the rooms of `with_room_ids` join it (grouped first), each gets the step's volume, then the favorite starts on it as Play now does. A room that does not join is logged and the others play. Each room handled counts as ran (each group for pause, stop and resume). A music step runs in full, as a scene's other steps do (ADR-054): whoever may run the scene (a member it was chosen for), and DirectorLink's own runs (schedules and links, which leave out only doors and gates), change every Sonos room the step names, grouped rooms included, whether or not the runner could control them; `FORBIDDEN` for a room is only for commands sent to `/v1/music` (*Who may*).
- **What a favorite step keeps.** Its id, and what the favorites list gives to start it: its name (`title`), address (`uri`) and description (`meta`), up to 1 KB, 2 KB and 8 KB, taken from the list DirectorLink read within the hour when the step is saved (the app sends only the id and name; when the list knows the id, what it has replaces what the request sent; when it does not, what was sent is kept, so a step sent back unchanged stays as it was). At run time the favorite is looked up in the favorites list: the same id with the same address (or the same name, when Sonos gave it a new address), else the same address under another id. What starts it is what the list has then: nothing a request sent ever reaches a player. A favorite no longer there is skipped with `FAVORITE_GONE` ("The Sonos favorite "…" is no longer in Sonos favorites") and nothing of the step runs; a shortcut only the Sonos app starts, `FAVORITE_NOT_PLAYABLE` (refused when saved, if known). The list read within the hour is used; while any scene plays a favorite, DirectorLink reads it every half hour (five minutes after a failed read), one request, so a run knows at once. Not read within the hour (just after a start): it is read first, the step counts as ran, and a favorite found gone is only logged.
- **Backups, the printout.** The steps go into backups as stored; a restore matches `with_room_ids` as a step's room (by id, else by name), leaving out a room that is gone. Composer's printout shows `play favorite "Example FM 99" at volume 35, grouped with Kitchen (10)`, `volume 15`, `resume`.
- **Going back to 1.7.0.** 1.7.0 knows only pause and stop: it leaves the other music steps out when it loads the scenes (and logs it), and its next save of the scenes drops them. As for refrigerator steps (ADR-049), 1.8.0 also keeps them apart, under `directorlink_scene_steps_2`, which 1.7.0 neither reads nor rewrites, and marks the scenes record `music_steps_kept`, which 1.7.0 and 1.6.0 drop when they save. Back on 1.8.0, a scenes record without the mark gets the steps back in their places (with refrigerator steps from 1.6.0, all in the order they had), in scenes with the same id that have none of them now; a step removed in 1.8.0 never comes back. A 1.7.0 app shows such a step as "Pause" (it knows only pause and stop) and sends it back unchanged when it saves the scene; it has no group card. A 1.8.0 app against a 1.7.0 driver shows neither groups nor the new actions (`features.sonos_groups`).

**Why:** Grouping is what the Sonos app does and what the owner expected; without it, playing in a second room takes the music away from the first. Two actions are the least that does it: `SetAVTransportURI` was allowed already, and `BecomeCoordinatorOfStandaloneGroup` takes no argument. The group's volume through each room's own volume needs no new action, and the balance kept the way the Sonos app keeps it means the group's slider does not flatten the rooms. A favorite looked up at run time, never started from what was stored, because the stored address came through the API; the address kept is what tells "removed" from "renamed". The list kept read only while a scene needs it, because ADR-044 reads the players only for someone.

**Consequence:** DirectorLink now changes which rooms play together: a room joined in the app or by a scene stays grouped until someone takes it out (in the app or the Sonos app). A scene that plays a favorite in the kitchen takes the kitchen out of the group it followed. Each scene favorite costs one Browse of the favorites every half hour while it exists. The cloud learns nothing new: these are sealed requests and scene steps on the controller (docs/ACCOUNTS.md unchanged). Tested on fake players answering with the real players' XML, the zone group state following the groups (`driver/tests/sonos_fake.lua`, `tests/sonos/fake-sonos.mjs`); grouping and Browse at run time have not been tried on the owner's players yet. No D1 migration.

## ADR-056 — Camera alerts from the DirectorLink · Hikvision drivers (since 1.10.0 from every camera driver of the agreement, ADR-065)

**Context:** The owner's 11 cameras run the DirectorLink · Hikvision Camera driver (`DirectorLink-Hikvision-Camera.c4z`, one per camera; the hub is `DirectorLink-Hikvision.c4z`, a separate repository). It turns each camera's detections into Control4 events and variables, and decides which of them are alerts for people: its event 1, **Alert**, fires when alerts are on for that camera and on the hub (its tile), not snoozed, and the detection matches the camera's **Alert On** (any detection, people and vehicles, or people only), once per alert (not again while it lasts, its Hold Time), with the variable `LAST_ALERT` (the detection's label: Person, Vehicle, Line Crossing, Intrusion, …) set just before. The family wants these on their phones, like a ring (ADR-050). Control4's own camera drivers have nothing like it.

**Decision (1.8.0):**
- **The driver's own Alert.** The camera adapter (`src/adapters/camera.lua`) recognises a camera proxy whose driver is `DirectorLink-Hikvision-Camera.c4z` (any case, and Composer's copy of a second download, `… (1).c4z`), watches that driver's event 1 and has the manager route it to the camera (`event_source_id`, as a DoorBird's events go to its doorbell). At the event it reads `LAST_ALERT` (by name, else id 1013: the 13th variable the driver adds) and keeps what the camera saw: `person`, `vehicle`, `face`, `motion`, `line_crossing`, `intrusion`, `region_entrance`, `region_exiting`, `tamper`, `scene_change`, `object_left`, `object_removed`, `alarm_input`, `pir`, or `other` for a label it does not know. The detection events themselves (Person Detected, …) are not watched: the Alert already applies the camera's Alert On, the hub's switch and its snooze, so DirectorLink adds no setting of its own for what counts. Other camera drivers: nothing is watched, nothing new is sent. *Since 1.10.0 (ADR-065)* any camera driver that follows DirectorLink's camera agreement (its marker `DIRECTORLINK_CAMERA`) is taken the same way, its `Alert` and `LAST_ALERT` found by name, with the labels `animal`, `package` and `license_plate` besides these; the Hikvision driver is still known by its file name until it sets the marker, by the marker only once it does.
- **A new kind, `camera`, sealed like the others** (`src/cloud/alerts.lua`, ADR-050): `{ kind: "camera", id, name, room, room_id, what, at, v }`, padded to the same 512 bytes, in a `notify` that is not brief (someone in the garden at night is still worth knowing a few minutes later). It goes to each key whose device switched alerts on, that chose camera alerts, and that may see that camera's pictures (`Access.canSeePictures(key, camera)`, src/auth/access.lua: per person, ADR-054). Off until chosen: cameras can be busy. At most one a camera a minute, and 30 camera alerts an hour of the controller's 60, so that busy cameras leave room for rings and doors. Not in the history.
- **Offered only where it means something.** `GET /v1/alerts/choices` lists `camera` (false until chosen) for a key when a camera of the project has such a driver (since 1.10.0, any driver of the camera agreement, ADR-065) and that key may see one of them; `features.camera_alerts` in `GET /v1/system` says the project has one. The app shows the switch "A camera sees a person, a vehicle or a line crossed" only then, with a line under it: off at first, each camera's Alert On in Control4 decides what counts (since 1.10.0: what each camera's driver sets, its Alert On on the Hikvision drivers), at most one a minute per camera.
- **The words and the tap.** The service worker says "Person at Garden at 21:14." ("אדם ב-גינה ב-21:14."), titled "Camera alert", one notification per camera (tag `camera-<id>`); words in English and Hebrew from the app, English in the worker when it has none. Tapping opens Cameras with that camera's full view (`#/cameras/<id>`, which becomes `#/cameras` once open, so Back does not open it again; a camera the device no longer has shows Cameras).

**Why:** The Hikvision driver's Alert is the family's own choice of what matters, set where they already set it (each camera's Alert On, the hub's tile and snooze, its schedules in Control4 programming); taking that one event keeps one switch in one place and needs no detection logic here. Sealed per key, as ADR-050, because a camera's name and what it saw are the home's. Off until chosen and limited, because eleven cameras that see motion can alert many times an hour, and the controller's 60 an hour (and the cloud's 60 `notify` a home an hour) must still carry a ring.

**Consequence:** What the cloud learns: one more kind of the same sealed notice (docs/ACCOUNTS.md "Who knows what"), not brief, for keys that may not be admin keys: a notice that is not brief and names a key that is not an admin key is now the refrigerator's door or a camera, no longer certainly the refrigerator; never which camera, what it saw or where; it sees more notices (at most 30 an hour from cameras). A 1.7.0 app with a 1.8.0 driver lists only the kinds it knows, so it shows no camera switch and its keys get no camera alerts unless a newer app on the same device chose them (its worker then shows the general words, opening History). A 1.8.0 app with a 1.7.0 driver: `features.camera_alerts` is missing, no switch. Going back to 1.7.0: its driver drops `camera` from the choices it loads and writes them without it at its next save (it knows no camera alerts; nothing it knows is lost); after updating again, camera alerts are off for those keys until chosen again. The choices are not in backups (as ADR-050). Not yet seen on a real controller: the Hikvision driver's Alert reaching another driver through `C4:RegisterDeviceEvent`, and `LAST_ALERT` read at that event (the driver sets it just before it fires); docs/TESTING.md's step checks both.

## ADR-055 — Camera pictures at the same time: four asked at once, eight fetched side by side, a camera's login kept

**Context:** On the owner's controller the Cameras grid is slow through the account. The app fetched its tiles one after another, each a full round trip through the relay (phone, Cloudflare, the home's connection, the camera, and back); the driver fetched at most 3 pictures at once in all, answered each camera's digest challenge anew (a 401, then the real request: two requests a picture) and kept no picture, so two tiles of one camera were two fetches. Already right, and kept: the app asks 320 px for a grid tile, 640 for the large one and 1280 or 1920 for the full view, and the DirectorLink · Hikvision Camera driver answers small widths with its sub stream's picture (Director's log: `ISAPI/Streaming/channels/102/picture`). The owner's own Hikvision driver sends one request at a time per camera because Hikvision refuses a nonce's counts that arrive out of order, which looks like a wrong password.

**Decision (1.8.0):**
- **The app** (`app/js/camera-feed.js`) asks for 4 pictures at once (`PICTURES_AT_ONCE`; `directorlink.picturesAtOnce`, 1 to 8, in a browser's storage overrides it, to compare), one request per camera and width however many tiles show it, live pictures (the doorbell banner) and the full view ahead of the grid's. A picture waiting or on its way is never asked for again, whichever loop asks (grid, banner, full view): it is joined. Each tile shows its picture as soon as it comes, also a tile drawn again meanwhile. A grid round waits for its pictures at most one refresh interval, so one slow camera does not hold the others back. The console says how long a screen of tiles took ("DirectorLink: 11 camera pictures in 2620 ms, 4 at once (remote)").
- **The controller** (`src/control4/camera.lua`) fetches at most 8 pictures at once in all, 2 from one camera's address and 3 from an address several cameras use (an NVR's channels); the others wait in the order asked, and one waiting for a busy address does not hold back another camera's. Up to 24 different pictures wait, and up to 16 requests for one picture; beyond, `503 CAMERA_BUSY` with Retry-After 1, as before.
- **A camera's login kept.** A digest challenge (realm, nonce, opaque, qop, algorithm) is kept per address and user and answered again with the nonce count going up, so a picture is one request instead of two. Each picture on its way to one address has a login of its own (its own nonce, used one request at a time, counts strictly in order). A 401, with `stale=true` or not, is answered once with its new challenge; refused again it is `CAMERA_LOGIN_FAILED` and the login is dropped, so a wrong password costs one attempt a picture, as in 1.7.0 (two, once, when the password changes while a login is kept). A login unused for 60 s starts again.
- **Shared pictures.** A picture (one camera at one size: the same address and user) asked for while it is on its way, or within 0.8 s of its arrival (`SHARE_MS`), is that picture: tiles of one camera, and several devices, share one fetch. Pictures are dropped 0.8 s after they came (a timer), so no more than what was fetched in the last 0.8 s is kept. *Refinement:* 0.8 s, not the 2 s first built: on the home network the doorbell banner and the full view ask again a second after each answer, and with 2 s every second ask got the kept picture, so the live picture changed every 2.6 s instead of 1.3 s.
- **Nothing waits on the Lua thread:** every request is Director's (`C4:url`), answered later. An error while a picture is asked for or its answer read (`C4:Hash` failing, a header of an odd shape) ends that picture as `CAMERA_UNREACHABLE`, logged, its login's challenge dropped and its place given back (the transfer's own time limit is over by then). At Debug level the log has a line per picture (`snapshot`: ms, requests, waiters, in_flight) for measuring on a controller.
- **Measured** (`driver/tests/test_cameras.lua`, `tests/app/camera-feed.test.mjs`, and the real app and driver on the fake Director, `scripts/dev_server.py --cameras 10 --latency <round trip> --camera-ms 150`, in headless Edge; 11 pictures: ten Hikvision cameras and the DoorBird's, from when the tiles appear until every one has its picture, median of three after the first):

  | | 1.7.0 | 1.8.0 |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | The controller's part, a request to a camera taking 150 ms (simulated) | 1,200 ms every time (3 at once, 2 requests each) | 600 ms the first time, then 300 ms (8 at once, then 1 request each) |
  | The app's part, each picture taking one round trip R (fake timers) | 11 R | 3 R |
  | App and driver, 300 ms round trip | 9.8 s (first time 9.8 s) | 2.9 to 3.0 s (first time 3.0 to 4.8 s, two runs) |
  | App and driver, 600 ms round trip | 16.6 s (first time 18.4 s) | 4.7 s (first time 4.9 s) |
  | Requests to the cameras for 11 pictures, after the first time | 21 | 11 |

  The fake Director seals in plain Lua (about 2 s for one picture), so in the browser the app asks with its key, as at a controller that does not seal; on a controller sealing is native. The integrator measures on the real controller through the account (docs/TESTING.md).

**Why:** Through the account the round trip is most of a picture's time, and the app's one-at-a-time made a grid of 11 cost 11 of them; 4 at once is 3, without flooding a phone's connection or the relay. On the controller the cameras' own time counts: 8 at once covers a grid in two rounds, and one request instead of two halves each. A login per picture in flight, rather than one shared nonce, because Hikvision checks each nonce's counts in order and refuses one out of order (the owner's own driver sends one request at a time per login for that reason, and keeps another login for its event stream). Sharing for 0.8 s, because a picture is the same for every tile and device that asks for it in the same moment, and a window shorter than the fastest refresh (a live picture at home, a second after each answer) gives each ask of one viewer a new picture.

**Consequence:** Up to 8 requests at once go to the cameras (up to 3 to an NVR), and a device asks the home up to 4 pictures at once; the relay already carries requests side by side and has no limit of its own for them. The controller keeps up to 8 pictures on their way and what came in the last 0.8 s in memory (a 1920 px picture is about 300 KB; the app asks 1920 only for one full view at a time). A 1.7.0 app with a 1.8.0 driver still asks one at a time, and its pictures still take one request each and are shared. A 1.8.0 app with a 1.7.0 driver asks 4 at once; that driver fetches 3 at once and lets 12 wait, so it answers them all, two requests each. Nothing is stored and nothing changes in the cloud. Not yet seen on a real controller and cameras: a Hikvision camera taking several kept nonces of one address at once, and how long it keeps a nonce (if it took only one at a time, each picture would go back to two requests, refused only as stale, never as a wrong password; the Debug log's `requests` shows it); docs/TESTING.md's step measures it.
## ADR-054 — Admins and members: two roles per person; an admin chooses each member's rooms, devices and scenes

**Context:** Up to 1.7.0 each API key has one of four ordered roles (ADR-025): `viewer`, `member`, `doors`, `admin`. A role is a device's, so one person's phone and computer can differ, and changing what someone may do means changing each of their devices. A role says how much, never where or what: a member controls every room and every kind of device and runs every scene, and a viewer sees every camera. The owner wants to give each person only some rooms, some kinds of devices and some scenes. And any admin can demote any other, the one who claimed the home too, and claim the home again, which moves it to another account and removes everyone else there.

**Decision (1.8.0):**
- **Two roles, set per person.** A person is a profile (docs/PREFERENCES.md). Every API key belongs to a person and has that person's permissions; all of a person's devices follow at once. **Admin**: everything; manages people (makes others admins or members, sets members' permissions), keys, invitations, rooms (names, order, hidden from members), scenes, schedules, settings, history, backups, remote access, the log. **Member**: what an admin chose for them (below). Members never edit scenes and never see schedules, history, keys, invitations, profiles, room settings, controller settings or backups.
- **A member's permissions**, set by an admin per member. *Rooms* they see: all, or a chosen list. *Device kinds* they use there: lights, climate (AC and heating), fans, blinds, music (Sonos), refrigerators, each on or off, all on at first; off hides that kind from them entirely (lists, Home, Turn off all) and the controller refuses it. Kinds combine with rooms: a light shows only if its room is theirs and lights are on. Heaters wired as KNX lights follow Lights. *Cameras* on or off: only cameras in their rooms. A doorbell in their rooms: its ring (and its ring alert) always, its picture only with cameras on, wherever its camera is placed. *Doors and gates* on or off: a member sees the doors and gates in their rooms, and their state, either way; opening needs doors on, and Door Control on in Composer. *Sees the alarm's status*: on by default (it still needs Alarm Status On in Composer). *Scenes they may run*: chosen by an admin, none for a new member; such a scene runs in full, devices they could not control themselves too, doors and gates included (the admin chose it). A new member (`POST /v1/api-keys` or an invitation with role `member` and `access`) has, for what `access` leaves out, every room and kind, cameras on, doors off, the alarm on and no scenes.
- **Rooms hidden from members.** An admin marks a room *Hidden from members* (`PATCH /v1/rooms/{roomId}` `{"hidden_from_members": true}`): the room and its devices disappear for every member, whatever their room list. Admins still see it, marked (`hidden_from_members: true` in `GET /v1/rooms`). Personal hiding (a profile's `hidden_rooms`) stays as it is, on top, for anyone.
- **Enforced on the controller, on every request.** `src/auth/access.lua` is the one module that answers; `src/auth/people.lua` keeps the records, in a new store `directorlink_people`. Lists are filtered: devices, rooms (`device_count` counts only what the caller sees), lights, thermostats, fans, blinds, cameras, relays, doorbells, refrigerators, music, scenes. A device, room or scene a member may not see answers `404` exactly like one that does not exist (the same code and words), so nothing leaks. A door or gate a member sees but may not open answers `403 FORBIDDEN`, as 1.7.0's missing `doors` role did. Admin-only routes answer `403 FORBIDDEN`. The route table (`routes.lua`) and the OpenAPI `x-directorlink-role` have two values: `member` (every person may ask; the handler answers with what they may see and do) and `admin`. `GET /v1/schedules` and `GET /v1/schedules/{id}` became admin-only. Turn off all (`POST /v1/off`): a member names only devices they control; any other is `400`, like a device that does not exist. Scene links and schedules run as DirectorLink itself: they control everything and never open doors or gates, as in 1.7.0. *Refinement (review):* nothing a member is sent names what they may not see.
